## 2026-11-15 08:35 UTC (Claude, data job): bank reserves, currency and the monetary base added to the Macro tab (new source, with the accounting identity and a weekly-against-monthly check)
- **Why this one:** the first queued candidate. Reserves are the standard measure of how much cash is in the banking system, and nothing on the tab showed them. I read each series page's frequency, units and licence lines first: `WRESBAL` and `WCURCIR` (weekly, ending Wednesday, in MILLIONS of dollars, H.4.1) and `BOGMBASE`, `MBCURRCIR` and `BOGMBBM` (monthly, in BILLIONS, H.6) all read "Public Domain: Citation Requested"; the older monthly currency series `CURRCIR` is marked DISCONTINUED on FRED, so I used `MBCURRCIR` instead. The units differ between the weekly and monthly series, which made the cross-check worth having.
- **What it shows (live, 2026-11-15):** for the week ending Wednesday 11 November 2026, bank reserves at the Fed of $2.96 trillion (up 1.0% on the week before, down 2.0% on four weeks ago, up 3.7% on a year ago; past-year low $2.86 trillion, high $3.14 trillion, 47% of its 53 weeks at or below) and currency in circulation of $2.49 trillion (up 2.9% on a year); the official monthly monetary base of $5.45 trillion in September 2026 shown as currency $2.48 trillion plus reserve balances $2.97 trillion (they add up to within $0.1 billion), up 0.7% on a month and down 0.5% on a year; and the cross-check that September's five weekly figures average $5,438.2 billion, 0.21% under the monthly base. New provider `reserves.py`, route `/api/macro/bank-reserves`, scheduler refresh, and a "Bank reserves and currency" card on the Macro tab under M2 money supply (source link, not a signal, not a forecast, demo mode shows nothing invented).
- **Checks, fixed from the real history before any test was written (full limits in `DATA_SOURCES.md`):** the strongest is an accounting identity the source itself states ("the monetary base equals currency in circulation plus reserve balances"): it holds to within 0.1 billion in all 225 months since 2008, so each of the last 12 months must match to within 0.5 billion. Also: weekly dates Wednesdays exactly 7 days apart and the same in both weekly series; weekly ranges; one-week moves outside the real record (reserves -8.8% to +15.9%, currency -0.6% to +1.8%); monthly months in a row with the base and currency moves inside the real record (-8.8% to +26.9% and -0.3% to +2.9%); and the weekly currency plus reserves, averaged over each of the last 6 whole months, within 10% of the monthly base (real: median 0.23%, largest 4.5% in March 2020). Real shocks are let through and pinned by a test (the March to April 2020 surge: base +24.8% in a month, reserves +15.9% in a week). A failing check shows nothing.
- **A check I removed:** my first draft also had range checks on the three monthly series. The breakage run showed no test could ever make one of them fire apart from the others (the weekly ranges, the move limits, the identity and the weekly comparison always fire first), so I took them out and said so in the source's notes instead of keeping code no test can prove. The lesson is now in `atlas-loop-rules`.
- **Verified:** 23 new backend tests; the hand-worked figures (the weekly changes, the 53-week range and rank, the base and its parts, the six-month cross-check) were recomputed a second way with pandas and match; 79 deliberate breakages of the provider each made a test fail (four could not be applied at first because my text match was ambiguous, and were re-run and caught); a live run through the real provider (2.0 s, the same figures); full backend suite 1667 passed (1644 plus 23); phone type check clean and 46 tests; a fresh web export; browser suite 48 of 48 with every live card present, so the new card's content checks ran; the card's screenshot read.
- **Not verified:** the Fed's release calendar (the page is blocked from here), so the 14-day weekly and 100-day monthly overdue lines are inferred; revisions (FRED says all data are subject to revision); that the weekly "week average" is averaged the same way as the monthly figures (the 0.2% typical gap suggests so; I did not read the Fed's definition); the card's one-line descriptions of reserves and currency, which paraphrase FRED's series titles and notes; the app on Windows.
- **Credibly profitable?** Not applicable to a data hour. Overall: seven experiments, seven fails; the search is paused; status: not yet.

## 2026-11-15 09:15 UTC (Claude, owner mission): btcquant steps 1 to 3 built and tested (data, indicators, account engine); every earlier breakage count re-run clean, with one correction
- **Why:** the owner's mission of today asks for an autonomous Bitcoin research and paper trading system with realistic persistent accounts that record every trade on signal. The rules were written and pushed first (`btcquant/PROTOCOL.md`, commit 1aea569); this is the first code under them. No strategy has been run on real data yet: the strategy notes and sources must be pushed first (step 4 of `btcquant/BUILD_PLAN.md`).
- **Built:** (1) `btcquant/data.py`: Coinbase BTC-USD candles (Binance, Kraken, Bitfinex and OKX all answer HTTP 403 from the network proxy; not worked around, see `DATA_SOURCES.md`), paged download, validation (sorted, unique, high and low consistent, positive prices, gaps reported never filled, moves over 40% flagged), only COMPLETE candles stored, a stored candle never overwritten, 4-hour candles built from complete hours, a manifest with the SHA-256 recorded in `btcquant/data/manifest.json`. Stored: 2,875 daily candles 2019-01-01 to 2026-11-14 with no gaps and 68,990 hourly candles with 9 small reported gaps. (2) `btcquant/indicators.py`: SMA, rolling standard deviation, Wilder ATR, RSI and ADX, Bollinger, realised volatility, with hand-worked tests. (3) `btcquant/account.py`: one step function used for both backtests and the future paper accounts: fills at the NEXT open, fee 0.10% and slippage 0.05% per side, size = the smaller of 1% of equity divided by the distance to the stop and 100% of equity, stops checked against the low (a gap fills at the open), same-candle entry and stop, entry rejected when the fill is at or below the stop, "armed" re-entry, a 15% circuit breaker that closes at the next open and halts until a human resets it, a weight mode for exposure strategies, and an order ledger that writes the order at the moment of the signal and the fill, rejection or stop later.
- **Verified:** 64 new tests (24 data, 10 indicators, 30 account, including the accounting identity cash plus position value = equity, fee and slippage arithmetic, gap and same-candle cases, and a state round trip). I broke the account engine on purpose 41 ways and every break made a test fail; one survivor came from my own test reaching the wrong reject path (the "stop equals the fill price" case), so I rewrote the test. Live download through the real provider, then validation: no fatal problem; the worst daily move in the file is -38.8% on 2020-03-12 and the best +19.5% on 2021-02-08 (both real).
- **Correction to earlier entries (found by re-running every breakage script from a clean state):** (a) Python had been reusing stale compiled files (same file size, same second), so a breakage run could report a broken file as healthy; the scripts now set `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1` and clear the caches (rule 11 in `atlas-loop-rules`). (b) Clean re-run results: natural gas 50 of 50 caught, M2 61 of 61, bank reserves 79 of 79 (four breakages had never been applied because the text to break also matched inside `WEEK_CUR_MOVE`; anchored to the line start, they are now applied and caught), the account engine 41 of 41. (c) **The 00:35 UTC entry on the failure-wording helper said "27 ways and each break made a test fail". That was wrong by one:** 26 breakages are caught, and the 27th (removing `^` from the HTTP status pattern) changes nothing, because `re.match` already anchors at the start of the text. It is an equivalent change, not a test gap; it is gone from the list. The board row for 00:35 was corrected to match.
- **Not verified:** (1) the account engine against any real exchange (no order was ever sent; it is a simulation, and the 15-minute delay between the 00:00 open and the 00:15 run is modelled as if the fill were at the open); (2) the hourly gaps are Coinbase's own (no trades or outages), not checked against a second venue; (3) Coinbase BTC-USD is not Binance BTC/USDT (USDT trades at a small premium or discount to the dollar and volume is smaller); (4) that the 41 breakages cover every way the engine could be wrong (for example nothing here proves the sizing rule is a good one, only that it does what the protocol says).
- **Credibly profitable?** No strategy has been run on real data under this system yet, so there is nothing to claim. Overall: seven earlier experiments, seven fails; the pre-registered `btcquant` protocol expects zero champions; status: not yet.

## 2026-11-15 09:21 UTC (Claude, owner mission): protocol v1.1 (four problems in my own pre-registered rules, found before any run), a 30-day breaker cool-off for research accounts, 14 strategy notes and a sources file, all pushed before any family runs
- **Why:** the rule I wrote for myself is "the note and the sources are pushed before a family runs". While writing the notes I asked of every gate whether the strategies it judges could ever pass it. Four answers were no. (a) The 15% circuit breaker halted an account for good after its first trip; a full-exposure Bitcoin account trips within months, so the weight-mode families and every full-exposure variant would have measured one trip and then a flat line. (b) "At least 30 trades" was an admission gate, so no slow rule (a 50/200 crossover, a 365-day momentum rule: a handful of trades in five years) could ever be admitted, and "cannot be judged" would have been reported as "no edge". (c) The "no breaker trip" gate was meaningless once (a) is fixed. (d) The drawdown clause of the TEST gate ("10 percentage points shallower than the exposure-matched hold") cannot be met by any strategy sized by the 1% rule, because the matched hold itself has under 10 points of drawdown. No result existed yet, so nothing was tuned to an outcome; it is recorded as amendment v1.1 with the reasons and a gate map (admission gates A1 to A6, promotion gates P1 to P6, and a third outcome, "too thin to judge").
- **Built:** `btcquant/PROTOCOL.md` v1.1; the account engine gained `cool_off_days` (research accounts resume by themselves at the close 30 days after a trip, flat, armed, peak re-based; the main portfolio still waits for a human) and `breaker_trips` (every trip kept even when the event log is off); a human reset now also re-arms the account and forgets the old target weight (it did not before, which would have left a weight-mode account flat after a reset: found while writing the cool-off tests). `btcquant/strategies/` has one note per family (hypothesis, why it might work, where it should work and fail, exact rules, sizing, grid, costs and risk limits, failure modes, an expectation written down before running, sources) for the twelve families and the two benchmarks, and `SOURCES.md`: 18 papers (the bibliographic record was confirmed on the day for 17 of them in EconPapers, RePEc, NBER, SSRN or the Econometric Society; for Faber 2007 the volume and pages were not; one, Zarattini et al. 2025, is a working paper) with the claims marked as the authors', plus the books, with what each does NOT show. Five families (RSI momentum, Bollinger, RSI oversold, volatility breakout, regime switch; 48 of the 100 trials) have no peer-reviewed test on Bitcoin that I could find, and the notes say so. The grid in each note is rendered from the code.
- **Verified:** 8 new account tests (38 in all, 72 for btcquant); 12 new deliberate breakages of the cool-off and trip logic, then the whole set of 53 run clean: 53 of 53 each made a test fail. Sources: bibliographic records only; no full text read.
- **Not verified:** the full text of any source (claims come from abstracts and summaries); Faber (2007) volume and pages, Faith (2007) publisher, Williams and Crabel titles and publishers, Kim-Tse-Wald journal details (all listed in `SOURCES.md`); the deflated Sharpe formula against the paper; whether 30 days is a good cool-off (it is a research convention; results will be shown with the trip count, and nothing here tests how sensitive they are to the 30).
- **Credibly profitable?** Nothing has been run on real data under this system. The protocol still expects zero champions. Status: not yet.

## 2026-11-15 09:30 UTC (Claude, owner mission): btcquant step 4 done: the twelve strategy families and two benchmarks as code (100 configurations), tested, no result looked at
- **Built:** `btcquant/strategies.py`: each family turns completed daily candles and one parameter set into a state (or a target weight) and a protective stop for every candle, using only candles up to that one. Donchian and the ATR trend filter replay their own trailing stop to know when they were stopped out, so their state matches what the account engine does with the same stop. `configs()` lists the 100 configurations, `neighbours()` the one-step neighbours used for the stability check, `spec_hash()` a canonical hash of a candidate (family, version, sorted parameters and the protocol constants that change behaviour, including the new cool-off).
- **Verified:** 54 new tests. Every family's rule is worked by hand on a tiny series, including the case where a comparison is exactly equal (a close equal to the highest high is not a breakout; a low equal to the stop touches it; RSI exactly at a threshold does not cross it; an ADX equal to its threshold is "ranging"; a move equal to k times the ATR is not a breakout). The grid counts match the protocol table (read from `PROTOCOL.md`, not retyped) and sum to 100; each family's note has the required sections and the same grid as the code. **No look-ahead:** for all 100 configurations, replacing every candle after day 300 (and again after day 520) with an unrelated random walk moved no earlier signal, state, weight or stop, while later signals did move in more than 50 cases, so the test can see a dependence when there is one. The replayed Donchian stop agrees with the account engine (signal at 4, fill at 5's open, stopped in candle 7 at the stop less slippage and fee). I broke the code on purpose 47 ways (one comparison, one constant, one window at a time) and each made a test fail. Nine first survived: seven were real gaps (a wiring test that the right windows are asked for, the equal-low and equal-close cases, a stop that bites on the candle after entry, a falling candidate that must not lower an ATR stop, the equal-averages case, a ranging entry whose averages say "down"); two were equivalent changes and are not counted: the Donchian "ratchet" cannot be seen to matter because the lowest low of the window cannot fall while a position survives (a property test checks the invariant), and the hash is protected twice against key order.
- **Not verified:** that these are the published rules (they are the simplified long-only, single-asset forms written in the protocol; the notes say how each differs); performance: no family has been run on real data under this system, by design; the 100-configuration count as the right N for multiple testing (the 15 crossover pairs are near copies of each other, so the true number of independent tries is smaller and the correction is conservative).
- **Credibly profitable?** Nothing run, nothing to claim. Status: not yet.

## 2026-11-15 09:43 UTC (Claude, owner mission): btcquant steps 5 and 6 done: the metrics and the backtest runner, both tested to the cent; still nothing run on real data
- **Built:** `btcquant/metrics.py`: total return, CAGR, maximum drawdown (from the running peak, the start counting as a peak), Sharpe and Sortino (daily, 365 days, risk-free zero; a ratio with a zero denominator is "undefined", never 0 and never infinite), Calmar, profit factor, win rate, average win and loss, expectancy, number of trades, holding period, turnover, exposure, fees and slippage paid, return against buy-and-hold and against an exposure-matched hold (a constant share of equity in Bitcoin rebalanced daily at the same costs), and the same by regime (BULL, BEAR, RANGE from the close against its 200-day average and that average against 20 days earlier; a day's return belongs to the regime at the previous close). `btcquant/backtest.py`: runs one configuration of one family over one period from a flat start with the account engine, exactly as the paper engine will; periods come from the calendar in the protocol and a period the data do not fully cover is refused rather than shortened. The account engine gained a fill counter (position changes for the weight-mode sample-size gates) and the share of equity held at each day's open.
- **Verified:** 20 metric tests (every ratio recomputed with Python's own statistics module or by hand, each undefined case, the exact-equality regime cases, the matched hold with and without costs, a whole-account example) and 19 runner tests: the TRAIN, VALIDATION and TEST periods hold exactly 1,096, 731 and 683 candles on the stored calendar; buy-and-hold equals the price ratio less one purchase's costs; cash is flat; a weight strategy worked by hand to the cent (a 25% average exposure, an 8.33% loss, the matched hold from its closed form); with costs it pays exactly the fee and slippage arithmetic; the full-exposure variant holds more; a risk-sized trade stopped out loses between 0.95% and 1.35% of equity (the planned 1% plus costs and gaps), never several times that; changing every candle after the period moves no result; the starting equity scales the account and leaves returns unchanged; a full-exposure account trips the 15% breaker, and the research account comes back after the 30-day cool-off while one without a cool-off waits for a human forever. I broke the code on purpose 48 + 32 ways and every break made a test fail (the first runs found one test whose data made two measures coincide, one slice that no test pinned, and one regime average that no test pinned; all now covered; one more change was equivalent and is not counted). The account engine's own 56 breakages (53 plus 3 for the new fields) are all caught on a clean run.
- **Not verified:** any number from real data (none was produced); that daily rebalancing at 0.15% is the right price for the matched hold (it is a stated convention, not a market quote); the interaction of the breaker cool-off with the results (30 days is a research convention, the trip count is shown beside every result).
- **Credibly profitable?** Nothing run, nothing to claim. Status: not yet.

## 2026-11-15 10:29 UTC (Claude, strategy job and owner mission): btcquant steps 7 to 10: validation, calibration on synthetic data (which sent the admission gates back for repair before they ever met the real data), registry, paper engine, daily runner and research run
- **The finding that matters.** I ran the whole research procedure on synthetic candles with the same calendar as the real data, where the truth is known: pure random walks with no skill (with and without upward drift) and series with a planted trend edge. The first admission gates (protocol 1.1) did not discriminate at all: they admitted at least one family in 75% of the no-skill datasets with drift and in only 17% of the datasets with a strong planted edge. They were measuring the market's drift, and Bitcoin's drift over 2020 to 2024 is huge, so on the real data they would have admitted several families whatever the truth. A raw deflated-Sharpe gate at 0.50 is a coin flip for the best of many trials, and on excess returns it was never reached even with a strong planted edge. I replaced the gates (protocol 1.2) BEFORE running anything on the real data: the strategy's returns are now measured against the exposure-matched hold (information ratio above zero on TRAIN and on VALIDATION), and the significance test is the random-timing p-value on VALIDATION alone, which is valid because the configuration was chosen on TRAIN (limit 0.05, chosen as the loosest on a grid that keeps the no-skill false-admission share near 15%). Calibration with the final gates (100 datasets per world, then 25 fresh ones): no-skill worlds admit something in 12% and 16% of datasets (20% and 28% on the fresh 25; pooled 14% and 18%); a strong planted edge in 60% (44% fresh, 57% pooled); a weak planted edge in 19% (barely above noise: a real but small edge is invisible in this much history). Everything is in `btcquant/CALIBRATION.md` with the limits of what it shows.
- **Built:** `validation.py` (selection that prefers a stable region to a peak, the stability gate, walk-forward with six-month windows, deflated Sharpe, the random-timing null, the admission and promotion gates with three outcomes: admitted, rejected, too thin to judge); `calibration.py` (four synthetic worlds, a runner and a summary); `registry.py` (every candidate logged with its canonical specification hash, renamed copies flagged at a position correlation of 0.90 and counted with the original, challengers and champions with frozen specifications that survive later amendments, a once-only look at TEST); `paper.py` (persistent virtual accounts, a ledger row written the moment a signal makes an order and again at the fill, rejection, stop-out or breaker, catch-up that is idempotent and refuses to step across a missing candle); `daily.py` and `research.py` (the 00:15 UTC run, the report and `dashboard.json`; the one-off research run that logs all 100 configurations, opens a paper account for each admitted family and refuses to run twice on the same protocol and data).
- **Verified:** 275 btcquant tests in all (108 new here). Parity: stepping an account one candle at a time with its state saved and reloaded each day gives the backtest's equity curve, trades, costs, stops and breaker state to the last digit, for nine families including a crash that trips the breaker. 79 + 29 + 20 + 42 deliberate breakages (validation, registry, paper engine, daily and research) each made a test fail, after the survivors were closed (they included: the equal-p boundary, the flat start of the timing null, a specification re-read through today's constants, an unrecorded stage). Nothing in this work has read the real candle files: every test and every calibration used synthetic data, so the real data and the TEST period are untouched.
- **Not verified:** anything about the real Bitcoin data (the research run has not been made); that the synthetic worlds resemble Bitcoin (they do not: no fat tails, volatility clusters or structural breaks); the power for a small real edge (the weak world suggests it is very low); the cool-off of 30 days as a modelling choice; the report text on a screen (there is no phone card yet).
- **Credibly profitable?** Nothing has been run on real data, so nothing to claim. Status: not yet. The protocol still expects zero champions.

## 2026-11-15 10:37 UTC (Claude, strategy job and owner mission): THE RESEARCH RUN ON THE REAL DATA, done once: 0 of 12 families admitted; no reliable edge established
- **What was run:** the pre-registered procedure (protocol 1.2, pushed before this run together with the strategy notes, the sources and the calibration) on 2,875 daily Coinbase BTC-USD candles to 2026-11-14 (hash `c9cb205d1d501e6a`): all 100 configurations on TRAIN (2020 to 2022) and VALIDATION (2023 to 2024), the chosen configuration of each of the 12 families, its neighbours, four cost settings, the full-exposure variant, six-month walk-forward windows to the latest candle, the deflated Sharpe ratios, and the random-timing null. It took 7.6 seconds. The TEST period was not used to choose or admit anything. The run refuses to repeat on the same protocol and data.
- **Result: nothing is admitted.** Eleven families are rejected and one (RSI oversold, 2 trades in five years) is too thin to judge. All eleven fail the same gate, A3: the random-timing p-value on the 2023 to 2024 validation days is above 0.05 (the lowest are 0.11 for the volatility breakout, 0.12 for time-series momentum and for momentum with a volatility filter, 0.16 and 0.17 for the ATR trend filter and the Donchian breakout; the crossover 0.40, RSI momentum 0.34; regime switch 0.86 and the trend filter exposure 0.95). Three also fail the stability or doubled-cost gates (Bollinger bands, the ATR trend filter, the volatility breakout fail stability).
- **What there is, honestly.** Eight families (the five trend and momentum rules, the volatility breakout, RSI momentum and volatility targeting) earned a positive information ratio against holding the same average share of Bitcoin all the time, on BOTH the 2020 to 2022 and the 2023 to 2024 days (for example time-series momentum 1.12 and 0.73, momentum with a volatility filter 0.88 and 0.73). That is the signature a weak edge would leave, and also the signature luck leaves about as often: the calibration (`CALIBRATION.md`) says that a strong planted edge is admitted about 57% of the time and pure noise about 14% to 18%, so an outcome of none admitted does not rule out a weak real edge, and it does not support one either. In the walk-forward (settings re-chosen every six months from 2022, scored on the next six months, windows that overlap the TEST period as pre-registered) the trend families' Sharpe ratios are 0.65 to 0.90 against 0.39 for holding over the same days, positive in 6 or 7 of 10 windows; nothing about this passed a gate.
- **Against holding, in raw returns, every strategy lost.** Over the 2023 to 2024 validation days Bitcoin rose 464%. The 1%-risk rules were invested only 3% to 9% of the time and returned 1% to 25%, the same as their exposure-matched holds (11% to 19%); the two exposure strategies (volatility targeting 298%, the 150-day trend filter 76%) held 74% and 62% and drew down 35% and 37%, tripping the 15% circuit breaker 12 and 13 times in five years. No rule beat buy-and-hold in return, and the ones that were invested a lot had large drawdowns. The higher Sharpe ratios of the low-exposure rules come from being invested little, not from earning more per unit risk than holding.
- **Paper trading.** The twelve chosen configurations are paper-traded forward as **monitors** (protocol 1.2a): not admitted, never promotable, kept so that every strategy's trades are recorded on signal and so the forward record can show whether rejecting them was right. The two benchmarks and the empty $100,000 portfolio (cash; no champion) are open. Six accounts have an order waiting for the next open (the three mean-reversion and regime entries, buy-and-hold, and two exposure changes); each was written to its ledger the moment its signal was made, with the numbers behind it.
- **Verified:** 278 btcquant tests; 179 deliberate breakages caught in all the modules; the run reproduces from the committed data, code and protocol (`python -m atlas_api.btcquant.research` refuses a repeat). **Not verified:** that any of this predicts the future; the data are Coinbase BTC-USD, not Binance BTC/USDT; costs are assumptions (0.10% fee, 0.05% slippage); the TEST period and everything after the data end (the forward paper record) are untouched.
- **Credibly profitable?** No. Nothing was admitted, nothing promoted, and the protocol's own expectation (zero champions) held. Status: not yet. The honest answer to the owner's question is that this research finds no reliable Bitcoin trading edge in these twelve simple rules over 2020 to 2024, and cannot rule out a small one.

## 2026-11-15 13:33 UTC (Claude, strategy job and owner mission): btcquant step 13 done: the research queue and its novelty pre-screen; six hypotheses from the literature, none a renamed copy; nothing was run
- **The job.** The 13 UTC strategy hour. The first unchecked step of `btcquant/BUILD_PLAN.md` was 13 (the research queue). It arrived while the owner's redesign request was being finished and waited its turn.
- **Found online** (a web search; abstracts and summaries only, the papers were not read in full, and quantpedia.com could not be fetched from here): the Monday effect in Bitcoin (Caporale and Plastun 2017, Aharon and Qadan 2019, Kahraman and Kök 2024, against Baur et al.'s finding of no persistent pattern); volume predicting Bitcoin returns except in bull and bear regimes (Balcilar, Bouri, Gupta and Roubaud 2017); nearness to the 13-week high (CXO Advisory's method; George and Hwang's stocks paper from memory); a volatility squeeze then a break (practitioner, no rigorous test found); short-term reversal after losing streaks (mostly intraday evidence); Chaikin money flow (practitioner). Time-of-day effects are left out (they need hourly candles and cost 0.30% a day).
- **The order of work is the point.** The six rules, their planned grids, the 0.90 renamed-copy line and a priority rule were written and pushed first (a07df08) together with the screen's code and tests; only then was the screen run on the real candles, and the results added. Nothing seen in the results could change the rules.
- **Built.** `atlas_api/btcquant/novelty.py`: the six rules as pure functions of completed candles (each tested on hand-made candles with the answers worked out by hand, and for look-ahead by moving every later candle and checking that nothing at or before day i changed); the screen, which correlates a rule's ON/OFF state with the state or target weight of all 100 configurations of the twelve families over the 1,827 TRAIN and VALIDATION days (no return, profit or Sharpe ratio is computed); a command that writes `reports/novelty.json` with the data hash. `btcquant/RESEARCH_QUEUE.md`: the procedure, the entries with sources and expected failures, the result and the engineering that must come before the first run.
- **Result.** None is a renamed copy. Highest correlation with any existing configuration: `monday` +0.02, `three_down` +0.12, `volume_surge` +0.44, `squeeze_break` +0.45, `money_flow` +0.49, `near_high` +0.69. The fixed priority rule gives the order monday, three_down, volume_surge, squeeze_break, money_flow, near_high. The calendar and reversal rules are almost uncorrelated with everything tested; the other four share 0.4 to 0.7 of their timing with a trend or momentum family, so if one had an edge it could be the trend edge already rejected.
- **Found while reading the code, not fixed here.** A protocol version bump (needed to add a family) would change every specification hash, because `strategies.spec` hashes `config.PROTOCOL_VERSION`, and account ids come from that hash: all fourteen accounts would get new ids and the daily run would open twelve duplicate monitors. Also `research.run_research` runs every family, so a one-family run needs a filter. Both are written into `RESEARCH_QUEUE.md` as the first engineering of step 14, before any entry is pre-registered.
- **Verified.** 32 new tests; 47 deliberate breakages of the module all caught (the first pass caught 45 of 48: one survivor was a redundant clause, removed; two were real gaps, a break of the middle band alone and the money-flow threshold, closed with new tests); the screen covers exactly the protocol's N = 100 configurations; the 0.90 line is tested on correlations of 0.9045 and 0.8165; the whole backend suite passes (1,995 tests, 3 minutes: the 1,963 before plus these 32).
- **Not verified.** That the sources say what their summaries say; that the correlation of state series tracks the correlation of the engine's sized positions (the registry repeats the 0.90 check on exposure when a candidate is logged); the exact 0.90 boundary (a correlation cannot be made to land on it); anything about returns, because none was computed.
- **Credibly profitable?** No. Nothing was run, so nothing can be claimed; the screen only says the six rules differ in timing from the twelve already rejected. My expectation, stated before any run: they will mostly be rejected (the Monday rule would need to earn more than 0.30% per Monday to pay its own costs). Status: not yet.
- **Next candidates.** Step 14: the engineering above, then pre-register and run `monday` once.

## 2026-11-15 16:38 UTC (Claude, strategy job and owner mission): btcquant step 14a: a protocol version bump can no longer disturb anything already registered; no family was added and nothing was run
- **The job.** The 16 UTC strategy hour. BUILD_PLAN step 14 (the queue one entry at a time, `monday` first) starts with the engineering that `RESEARCH_QUEUE.md` listed after the last strategy hour found that the protocol version is hashed into every specification hash, and so into every paper account's id. This cycle did the first two items and the reading of several runs as one record.
- **Built (protocol amendment v1.2b, engineering only; no gate, threshold, grid or result changed).** Each family carries the protocol version it was pre-registered under (`Family.since`, 1.2 for the twelve) and that goes into its specification hash, so bumping the version changes no existing hash or account id. `run_research` evaluates only the families of the version it runs under (a version that added none raises `NothingToRun`; the earlier results stand and are never run again) and records which families it covered; the trial count N counts every configuration of every family. `research.merged` reads the runs of all versions as one record (newest outcome per family, every admitted candidate once, the list of runs), and the dashboard, the daily report and the phone view use it: verdict details are read from one report file per version, oldest first, a later run winning, and a damaged file loses only its own families. Report and command texts that said twelve now count.
- **Verified.** `tests/test_btcquant_versions.py`, 14 tests. All 100 configurations of the real run and all 15 real paper accounts keep their hashes and ids under a bump to 1.3 and to 2.0; a toy family under 1.3 carries 1.3 in its hash; and a whole two-run world is tested end to end through `run_research`, `run_daily` and the phone view (a noise world, protocol 1.2 for the twelve families, then 1.3 for the toy family): only the toy family is evaluated (N = 102), the first report file is byte for byte unchanged, exactly one account is opened and none duplicated, the dashboard has thirteen outcomes and two runs, the daily report names both report files, the phone view keeps all twelve old verdicts with their reasons and adds the new one, and the headline says "research runs up to". **31 deliberate breakages all caught** (the first pass skipped one, a mistyped mutant, and let one through: the command's "of 12 families" sentence, which a test with a one-family run now covers). Four existing tests changed because they had encoded the old behaviour: two expected a version bump to move a hash, one expected every version to re-run all families, and one expected the daily report to say "twelve accounts" for a world with two monitors, which was simply wrong. Whole backend suite: 2,021 passed (3 minutes). A read-only check against the real committed lab: the merged record has the 12 outcomes and one run, and the phone view's headline is unchanged.
- **Not verified.** The real daily run after this change (it runs at 00:15 UTC and will add a `runs` list to `dashboard.json`; it was not run early, so as not to change committed state outside its schedule); a real second research run (no new family exists yet); the phone's wording for several runs ("Admission bar, research run of ...", which stays as it is until a second run exists).
- **Credibly profitable?** No. Nothing was run. Status: not yet.
- **Next candidates.** Step 14b: restate the calibration for thirteen families, then pre-register `monday` (note, amendment v1.3, the family with tests, a logo code in `view.SHORT`), push, then run it once.

## 2026-11-15 19:50 UTC (Claude, strategy job and owner mission): btcquant step 14b: the first queued hypothesis, the Monday effect, pre-registered under protocol v1.3 and run once: `monday` rejected
- **The job.** The 19 UTC strategy hour: BUILD_PLAN 14b, the first entry of the research queue (`monday`, a calendar rule: hold Bitcoin only through Mondays, UTC). The pass through the plan's list took the whole hour: work out the gates, calibrate, pre-register and push, run once, log.
- **Worked out before any code (as the first lesson of the protocol says).** As written, two gates could not be passed by a weekday rule: A4 (parameter stability needs neighbours; a weekday has none, so every calendar rule failed it by construction) and, in part, A2 and A5 (a weekly round trip costs 0.30%, 0.60% at doubled costs, so only a Monday premium above that can show). A3's random-timing test shifts the schedule by whole days, so for a weekly rule one shift in seven is a near-copy of the rule: harder for a real effect, not easier for a false one (checked below). Decisions, fixed in amendment v1.3 before the run: one configuration (Monday) instead of the queue's planned seven (every source claims Monday only; a weekday is a category so neighbour-smoothed selection and A4 mean nothing; six more configurations would raise N for no hypothesis; N = 101), weight mode (no stop, the whole account through the Monday candle), and A4 for a one-setting family asks only that its own TRAIN Sharpe ratio is above zero (reported as "not applicable (one setting)"). No family of 1.2 and no gate, threshold or grid of the twelve changed; every specification hash and paper account id is unchanged.
- **Calibration on synthetic data (restated for thirteen families; no real data touched).** `calibration.run_family_world` runs one family alone through selection and the gates (0.1 s a dataset), with new worlds that pay a planted premium on every Monday candle. On 1,000 datasets per world: the new family was admitted in 1 and 2 of the no-skill worlds (0.1%, 0.2%), its timing p-value was at most 0.05 in 4.9% and 4.6% of them (so the weekly schedule does not break the test), and with a planted premium of 0.3%, 0.6% and 1.0% a day it was admitted in 5.7%, 29.4% and 70.8%. The share of no-skill datasets with something admitted among the thirteen is at most the old share plus one point: still about one in six. Written into the note, the amendment and `CALIBRATION.md` before the run: a rejection will mean that no premium of about 0.6% a day or more was present, not that there is no Monday effect.
- **Built (tests first).** `strategies.weekday` and the family (weekday numbering checked against the calendar's own answer including the second before midnight, the wrap-around for all seven days, no warm-up, prices play no part, a hand-worked cost chain through the account engine where a flat price loses exactly four round trips of costs), the A4 rule, protocol version 1.3, the phone view's logo (MON), weekday names and wording for a one-setting family, grammar for one-family reports, the novelty screen compares with the twelve families it was written against. Older tests that assumed twelve families under version 1.2 now pin the version they describe, and the toy family of the version tests moved to a hypothetical 1.4.
- **The run (once, 19:45 UTC, after the pre-registration was pushed at 19:45; 2,875 candles to 2026-11-14, hash `c9cb205d1d501e6a`).** **Rejected** by A2, A3 and A5 (A1 passed with 482 position changes; A4 not applicable and passed on TRAIN Sharpe 0.31). TRAIN 2020-2022: +16.1% after costs against +21.8% for holding the same average 13% of Bitcoin all the time and +1,303% for buy-and-hold; Sharpe 0.31; maximum drawdown 40.3%; 4 circuit-breaker trips. VALIDATION 2023-2024: +17.5% against +30.3% and +464%; Sharpe 0.45; drawdown 26.7%. Information ratio against the matched hold +0.09 and -0.12 (A2 needs both above zero); random-timing p 0.284 (174 of 612 shifted copies did as well); Sharpe at doubled costs -0.08. Fees alone were $3,932 and $2,123 on a $10,000 account; the same rule returned +50.8% and +38.1% at 0.07% a side and -20.6% and -8.5% at 0.30%. The pre-registered expectation held.
- **Exploratory context (after the result, TRAIN and VALIDATION days only, not a test, nothing decided from it).** The average Monday candle returned +0.46% and +0.52% against +0.10% and +0.23% on other days: a difference of +0.36 and +0.30 points with standard errors of 0.39 and 0.34 (t about 0.9 each; 1.2 pooled); the Monday medians are +0.14% and +0.04% and Wednesday is as high as Monday. A difference of about +0.33 points a Monday is about the weekly cost, so even at face value it would be a wash after costs.
- **Verified.** 360 btcquant tests and the whole backend suite pass (2,077 before the last test additions; re-run below). **45 deliberate breakages of the new code all caught** (the first set caught 41; the four survivors were three real gaps in the calibration tests (a premium out by 0.1 point passed on seed luck, an outcome forced to "admitted" passed, a family argument was ignored) and one breakage that had been run against the wrong test file; each fixed and re-run). A dry run of the daily pipeline on a copy of the real state: it opens `monday-9db66957` as a monitor (13 monitors, 16 accounts), reads both runs as one record, and the phone view shows "Day-of-week holding (Monday)", logo MON, "Rejected" and three plain-word reasons.
- **Not verified.** The real daily run at 00:15 UTC after this change (only the dry run on a copy); the weekday studies beyond their abstracts and the size of the premium they report; any Monday effect outside the tested design (another time zone, hourly candles, other exchanges); the phone screen itself (no mobile code changed).
- **Credibly profitable?** No. A rejection of a calendar rule; nothing here is evidence that any rule makes money. Status: not yet.
- **Next candidates.** BUILD_PLAN 14c: `three_down` (short-term reversal; the order is fixed by the priority rule), the same pass: gates by hand, calibration alone on synthetic worlds with and without a planted effect, note and amendment v1.4, family with tests, push, run once.

## 2026-11-15 20:45 UTC (Claude, data job): Bitcoin miner revenue (mempool.space), a new section of the Bitcoin network card
- **Source added.** What miners were paid for the last 144 blocks, from mempool.space's reward statistics (`/api/v1/mining/reward-stats/144`; free, no key, the same host and terms as the network card of cycle 6 and the pool shares of this morning). Checked live at 20:18 UTC: HTTP 200 in about a second, blocks 975,868 to 976,011.
- **What it shows (live, 2026-11-15).** For the last 144 blocks (about a day): miners were paid 451.73 BTC in all, of which 450.00 BTC was block subsidy (3.125 a block) and 1.73 BTC transaction fees, so fees were 0.38% of what miners earned; 821,183 transactions were confirmed (about 5,703 a block, 211 satoshis of fees each on average); the next halving is at block 1,050,000, 73,989 blocks away, about 514 days (2028-04, an estimate at ten minutes a block), when the subsidy falls to 1.5625 BTC. **Where:** BTC tab, Bitcoin network card, "What miners were paid".
- **Why this source today.** It was the candidate noted last cycle: Bitcoin-specific, free, and with a built-in exact check. FRED answered again at 18:2x, but this source was already checked live, and the FRED candidates (retail sales, housing starts, industrial production) are queued for the next data cycle.
- **What the live probes found, and what was left out.** The source ignores the block count in its address: 10, 50, 144, 1,008 and 4,320 all answered with the same last 144 blocks, so no week or month totals exist from it. Its timelines of average fees and rewards hold an exact per-point identity (the average reward minus the average fee equals the subsidy, in all 1,413 points of the last month) but they are samples, not totals: over the same 144 blocks their block-weighted averages were 8% (1 week), 14% (1 month) and 2% (3 months) away from the exact figure, so a week or a month of fee share would have been an estimate with no check that could confirm it, and it is not shown.
- **Checks built in, with limits set from the real answers before any test.** The window is exactly 144 blocks; reward above zero, fees between zero and the reward, at least one transaction a block; and the identity reward minus fees = the sum of the subsidies of those exact blocks, worked out from the halving schedule (50 BTC shifted right once per 210,000 blocks, the same integer shift as Bitcoin's code; a test sums the rule over every era to the known supply of 20,999,999.9769 BTC), to within 1,000 satoshis (the real answer is exactly 0). A failing check or request shows nothing and loses only this section. A block that claimed less than it was owed (rare, but it has happened) would hide the section for a day.
- **Verified.** 22 new tests in `test_onchain.py` (35 in the file) on the saved real answer and made-up windows: the subsidy at every halving boundary and over the whole supply, windows across one and two halvings, every figure worked by hand (and a busy day of 100 BTC in fees worked by hand), every check at its boundary and the refusal one step over, the next halving for several heights, a missing or unreadable field, the request, the failure in words, the cache lifetime for a failing section, demo and the route. **46 deliberate breakages all caught** (the first set of tests caught 42; of the four survivors one was a rounding that hid a wrong denominator, one a boundary that cannot be reached before the 33rd halving, and two an error message not pinned; each now has a test). Phone: `tsc` clean, 72 unit tests. Browser suite: **49 of 49 checks pass and the new section's content checks ran against the live source** (the run lists fourteen Treasury FiscalData and CFTC cards as skipped because their live cards were unavailable at that moment, not this one); the healthy scenario run again after adding a screenshot of the section: 31 of 31; I read the screenshot (the window, the three rows, the fee share, the transactions, the halving with its date marked as an estimate). Whole backend suite: 2,093 passed in 3 minutes 20.
- **Not verified.** That mempool.space's reward statistics are right in the first place (the identity shows they are consistent with the halving schedule, which is a different thing); the longer windows (the source does not give them); that a changed window or unit on the source's side would be caught in every case (the window, range and identity checks cover the cases tried).
- **Credibly profitable?** Not applicable to this job (a data card describes miner pay, not a signal). Status: not yet.
- **Next candidates.** The FRED candidates now that it answers (retail sales, housing starts, industrial production); the ECB's euro-area consumer prices and money supply.

## 2026-11-17 04:42 UTC (Claude, strategy job; owner mission): btcquant step 14c, `three_down` pre-registered under protocol v1.4, then run once: REJECTED
- **What was done.** The second hypothesis of the research queue, the short-term reversal rule `three_down` (buy at the open after `streak` lower closes in a row, sell at the open after the first close that is not lower; `streak` 2, 3 or 4), became family 14 and was pre-registered and pushed at 04:38 UTC (commit 3da34a8), then run once on the stored candles to 2026-11-16 with every gate as written: **rejected** by A2, A3, A4 and A5 (A1 passed with 213 trades). Run of 2 chosen on TRAIN, no setting had a positive TRAIN Sharpe ratio. 2020-2022: -1.7% against +2.0% for holding the same average exposure (1.2% of the account) every day and +1,303% for buy-and-hold; 2023-2024: -0.9% against +3.9% (and +464%). Before fees and slippage the account was up $83 and $157 on $10,000; costs of $249 and $248 more than took it. Timing p 0.77 on VALIDATION, Sharpe -0.72 at doubled costs, walk-forward -0.36 against 0.39 for holding. Exploratory context (TRAIN and VALIDATION only, not a test): the average candle after a run of lower closes earned only +0.07 to +0.17 points more than other candles (t 0.3 to 0.4), below the +0.15 points a candle that a trade costs. Result and context in `btcquant/RESEARCH_QUEUE.md`, record in `reports/research_1.4.md`.
- **Before the run (all pushed first).** Worked out by hand and then measured on synthetic data whether every gate can be passed by this kind of rule (`CALIBRATION.md`, fourteen families, three new reversal worlds, 1,000 datasets a world): yes; false admission 0.8%, power 5.7% / 15.9% / 38.3% at a planted premium of 0.3% / 0.6% / 1.0% a day, the timing gate A3 (VALIDATION alone, 20 to 90 trades) being the one that limits it and A4 not an obstacle; the procedure picks an end setting (2 or 4) in 93% to 99.6% of datasets; the share of no-skill datasets with something admitted among fourteen families is at most 16.0% and 21.6% (it was 14.4% and 19.2% among thirteen). Decided before any run: stop-based mode (3 x ATR stop, 1% risk sizing, like the other two mean-reversion families) rather than a whole account, and the exact meaning of a lower close. Amendment v1.4 changes no gate.
- **Engineering.** `down_streak` and the family written test-first (hand-worked series, an equal close and the first candle at the boundary, a plain-loop reference on random walks with ties, the no-look-ahead test over every configuration, the exact fill rows and prices through the account engine, and a second definition in the novelty screen that must agree); version 1.4; view wording ("3 lower closes in a row") and logo REV; reversal worlds in the calibration. 52 deliberate breakages of the new code: 49 caught at first, the three survivors an equivalent clause, a dead guard (removed) and a name pinned only in another test file (now pinned in the registration test). Older tests no longer name the current version or trial count (they compare with `C.PROTOCOL_VERSION` and `S.n_configurations()`; one test names the pair and checks `PROTOCOL.md`), the toy family of the version tests moved to a hypothetical 1.9, and the test of the committed artifacts no longer assumes twelve monitors (the daily run opened the `monday` one). The skills `atlas-experiments` (engineering traps) and `atlas-loop-rules` (an after-an-outage rule) were updated.
- **An outage, and what was done about it.** This session's worker was down from about 22:30 UTC on 2026-11-15 to 04:17 UTC on 2026-11-17, so 31 triggers arrived at once (29 hourly cycles and 2 daily runs). This entry is the CURRENT hour's job only; the missed improve, skills and data hours were not replayed. The daily btcquant run was caught up from a throwaway checkout of the pushed code (so the report could not name the unpushed protocol 1.4): two candles added (2026-11-15 and 2026-11-16, close $64,816.79), every account stepped through both in order, buys filled at the 2026-11-15 open ($64,150.92) in four accounts, no stop and no halt, the `monday` monitor opened (commit 305dc63).
- **Verified.** The whole backend suite (2,111 tests) before the run and the 378 btcquant tests after it, including the tests of the committed artifacts; the calibration on 5,250 synthetic datasets; the mutation pass above. **Not verified:** that the daily run at 00:15 UTC on 2026-11-18 opens the `three_down-e7923e48` monitor (not run yet); the reversal studies beyond their summaries; any reversal effect outside the tested design (hourly candles, other exchanges, other stop rules); the phone screen (no mobile code changed).
- **Credibly profitable?** No. A rejection of a reversal rule; nothing here is evidence that any rule makes money. Status: not yet.
