## 2026-11-17 05:36 UTC (Claude, data job): US retail and food services sales (Census Bureau via FRED), a new card on the Signal Macro tab
- **Source added.** What American shops, online stores, restaurants and bars sold each month, from the Census Bureau's Advance Monthly Retail Trade Survey through FRED: `RSAFS` (retail and food services, total), `RSXFS` (retail trade) and `RSFSDP` (food services and drinking places), seasonally adjusted, millions of dollars (`fred.stlouisfed.org/graph/fredgraph.csv?id=...`, free, no key, a plain client as for the other FRED cards). Checked live at 05:18 UTC: HTTP 200 on all three in 0.3 to 0.5 s, 417 monthly values each from January 1992 to September 2026, none missing. The licence line on each series page reads "Public Domain: Citation Requested" (read at 05:2x), so the card names the Census Bureau and links the series page.
- **What it shows (live, 2026-11-17).** September 2026: $745.0 billion, up 0.8% on August, 1.7% on three months ago and 6.3% on a year ago; retail trade $652.1 billion (87.5% of the total, up 6.2% on the year) and food services and drinking places $92.9 billion (12.5%, up 7.0%); over the past ten years yearly growth ran from -19.5% (April 2020) to +51.5% (April 2021) and 79% of those months grew slower than now. The card says the newest month is an advance estimate that is revised in the next release and that the dollars are not adjusted for price changes. **Where:** Signal, Macro tab, right after the jobless claims card.
- **Why this source today.** It was the first FRED candidate queued, FRED answered again, and it has the strongest built-in check of the candidates: retail trade plus food services equals the total, which holds exactly (difference 0) in all 417 real months, so a half-updated or mixed-up download cannot pass. Housing starts (which also have an exact parts identity: 1,221 = 859 + 13 + 349 in September, exact in all 813 months) is queued next; industrial production has no such check.
- **Checks built in, with limits set from the whole real history before any test.** Totals between 100,000 and 2,000,000 million dollars (real 158,647 to 745,021); the last 24 months in a row; no month moved outside -25% to +30% (real extremes -14.18% in April 2020 and +19.42% in May 2020, so the 2020 shock is never refused); no yearly growth outside -30% to +60% (real -19.51% and +51.49%); parts adding to the total within 1 million dollars (real: exactly 0); food services 5% to 20% of the total (real 7.40% to 12.66%, which also catches swapped parts); a part that is missing, or a series that ends in another month, is refused. A failing check or request shows nothing and the failure is cached for two minutes. Marked OVERDUE when the newest month is more than 90 days old by its date (77 today).
- **Verified.** 27 new tests in `test_retailsales.py` on the saved real answer and made-up series: every figure worked by hand and again in plain Python before the module was written, every check at its boundary and one step over, each window's edge, the 2020 shock and the 2021 rebound that must not be refused, the overdue boundary, the cache lifetimes for a success and a failure, the failure in words, demo, the route, and the plain-client rule. **93 deliberate breakages: 85 caught by the first tests and 90 after new tests for five of the eight survivors (the window of the first-of-the-month check, a missing retail part, the largest difference over the window, and a fall of exactly 25%); the last three survivors are an inclusive-versus-exclusive edge at exactly +30% a month, +60% and -30% a year, which no level can reach (a ratio of 1.3, 1.6 or 0.7 is not exact in floating point), so they are equivalent in practice.** Backend: 2,138 passed (the 2,111 of before plus these 27); phone: typecheck clean, 72 unit tests; browser suite 49 of 49 with this card's live content checks run (the card loaded live data in the browser); the screenshot (`07w-retail`) was read: the card matches the light look and the live numbers. A first attempt to apply the phone edits as one long shell command was refused by a safety check (it contained no deletion; the regex-heavy script confused the parser) and did not run; the same edits were made with the file tools.
- **Not verified.** That the Census Bureau's advance estimates are right (the card says they are revised); the card on a real phone; that the October figure arrives on schedule (the release date is not in the app's calendar).
- **Credibly profitable?** Not applicable to this job (a data card describes consumer spending, not a signal). Status: not yet.
- **Next candidates.** Housing starts with its three parts (`HOUST`, `HOUST1F`, `HOUST2F`, `HOUST5F`; read the parts' licence lines first); the ECB's euro-area consumer prices and money supply; industrial production if a check can be found.

## 2026-11-17 06:50 UTC (Claude, improve job): an accessibility probe for the Signal phone app (`axe-core` plus two checks of our own), then the fixes it found
- **What was done.** The backlog item found at the 2026-11-15 skills hour. `mobile/e2e/signal.e2e.cjs` has a new `a11y` scenario (also part of the default run; `node e2e/signal.e2e.cjs a11y` alone takes about 45 seconds) that opens 11 screens (the connect screen, watchlist, an asset page, Settings, BTC, Journey, Macro, Discover, Journal, Search, and search results) and on each runs `axe-core` 4.14.0 with the WCAG 2.x level A and AA rules (it runs inside the page Playwright already opens, no network), and two checks of our own: every tappable element is at least 44 by 44 points (the tabs you are not on stay mounted and are marked `aria-hidden`; they are skipped), and every labelled control's spoken name contains the words it shows, in order (WCAG 2.5.3 "label in name"). It fails the run on any new violation or any new small target. A self-test puts a 20 by 20 button whose label does not match its text, and a button with no name at all, on the page and checks that the probe reports all three, so a clean result means something. Dependency check done before adding it: `package-lock.json` gained only the `axe-core` entry (dev only, MPL-2.0, no install script, no `os` or `cpu` limits); `npm ci --dry-run` is fine; `npm audit` shows the same 3 moderate findings as before, none about axe.
- **Found, before any fix (first run, report only).** A critical `aria-required-parent` on all six tabs (they were `role="tab"` with no `tablist` around them); the spoken labels of the watchlist rows and of the mini Bitcoin quote did not hold the words the row shows (the trend line "Downtrend · -0.9% in 1M" and the absolute change were never spoken, and the quote said "Bitcoin" where it shows "BTC"); the Journey card row was a scroller no keyboard could reach; and 2 to 24 tap targets under 44 by 44 points on every screen (round header buttons 40, filter chips 31 high, collapsible card headers 35, text buttons 37, search boxes 40 to 42, the Settings close button 34 by 36, the search "clear" cross 18 by 20). The connect screen was clean. Colour contrast: axe found no failing text on any screen.
- **Fixed, in shared components (73 lines added and 40 changed in the app, 131 lines of tests: the `a11y` scenario and 4 new unit tests).** The tab bar groups its tabs in a `tablist`; `TAP = 44` in `components/ui.tsx` (round header buttons, icon buttons, text buttons, chips, the Add symbol pill, collapsible headers, the mini quote bar, both search boxes, the clear cross); a chip keeps its drawn size (31 high) inside a 44 high button, so the look stays and the touch area grows; round logos are now drawn beside a row's button instead of inside it (`LogoSlot`, which lets touches through), so their letters ("BTC", "AA") are not part of what the button says; the watchlist, search, Journey strategy and agent rows build their spoken label from the words they show, in the order they show them, with the freshness or the tap's effect added after (`spoken()` in `lib/format.ts`); the Journey card row can take keyboard focus. Not changed on purpose: the three header buttons look the same except that they are 44 points instead of 40.
- **One decision worth knowing.** axe's own "label in name" rule (`label-content-name-mismatch`) is switched off and replaced by our word-by-word check, because axe glues the text of separate blocks together without spaces ("BTCCryptoBitcoin...") and react-native-web draws every `Text` as its own block, so the rule flagged a label that did contain every visible word. The replacement compares the words of the page's own `innerText` with the words of the label and found the agent rows (the stance word "Supportive" was visible and never spoken) and the Journey section toggle that axe's rule had not.
- **Checks.** `tsc` clean; unit tests 76 passed (72 before, 4 new); the web export builds; the full phone e2e passed 62 of 62 (49 before, 13 new), with every live card answering so every content check ran; the screenshots of the watchlist, Journey and Macro were read after the change (logos beside the rows, filter chips unchanged in look, header buttons slightly larger). Backend untouched.
- **Break-your-own-checks.** Nine deliberate regressions, one at a time, each put back into the app and run through the `a11y` scenario alone (tabs without a tablist; the tap constant at 40; the watchlist label with the type before the symbol; the Journey scroller without keyboard focus; the Journey section toggle label without its title; the mini quote saying "Bitcoin"; the search box at its old 42 points; chips at their old 31; the agent row label without the stance word). **All nine were caught**: the scenario failed on the screens where each regression belongs (for example the tablist one on every tab screen, the search box one on Search and search results only), none survived; each file was put back and its hash checked, and the export was rebuilt from the restored sources.
- **Known and queued, not fixed.** Every card ends with a one-line `Source: ...` link that is 15 points high (35 files repeat the same inline button); the probe's short list `A11Y_KNOWN_SMALL` allows exactly these and nothing else. Fixing them needs a decision about the extra height (about 29 points under every card) or a hit area that reaches into the card padding, so it is left for a cycle of its own.
- **Not verified.** A real screen reader (VoiceOver or TalkBack) on a phone; the native build at all (the app was checked as the web export in a 390 by 844 browser; `hitSlop` is ignored on the web, so the probe measures the drawn button, and on a phone the round buttons keep their extra slop as a bonus); keyboard focus order and visible focus rings; text scaled to 200%; the reduced-motion setting; colour contrast of text over a gradient (axe cannot measure it and says so: about 14 items on the watchlist, 12 to 22 on other screens, printed as "text axe could not measure"; the theme's own contrast test covers the card colours).
- **Credibly profitable?** Not applicable to this job (an accessibility check says nothing about a signal). Status: not yet.

## 2026-11-17 07:50 UTC (Claude, strategy job; owner mission): btcquant step 14d, `volume_surge` pre-registered under protocol v1.5, then run once: REJECTED
- **What was done.** The third hypothesis of the research queue, the volume rule `volume_surge` (a day whose volume is at least `ratio` times the mean of the 20 days before it and whose close is above the one before: buy at the next open and hold `hold` candles, a new surge day inside the hold starting the count again; ratio 1.5, 2 or 3, hold 3, 5 or 10), became family 15 and was pre-registered and pushed at 07:38 UTC (commit 235a4b2), then run once the same minute on the stored candles to 2026-11-16 with every gate as written: **rejected** by A3 and A4 (A1, A2 and A5 passed). Chosen on TRAIN: ratio 1.5, hold 10 (the busiest corner). After costs on $10,000: +13.5% in 2020-2022 and +15.0% in 2023-2024 on 29 and 27 trades and 3% and 6% of the account, against +4.8% and +13.0% for holding the same average amount of Bitcoin every day (information ratio 0.94 and 0.31); Sharpe 1.26 and 1.63, 1.34 at doubled costs; but the timing on 2023-2024 was no better than random timing (p 0.28) and the two neighbouring settings reached 68.5% and 66.9% of its TRAIN Sharpe against the 70% needed (a near miss). That is a profile like six of the twelve first families (A3 alone) and like its closest relative `vol_breakout` (A3 and A4), not a new best. The result and its exploratory context (the candle right after a surge day earned no more than other candles; the held candles' averages are within chance) are in `RESEARCH_QUEUE.md`.
- **Before the run (all pushed first).** The synthetic worlds carried a constant volume of 100, so a volume rule could never fire in them: every world now has a volume series (log-AR(1), persistence 0.7, innovation 0.4) drawn from a random stream of its own, so no price, wick or return of any world moved and no earlier calibration figure changes (tests); its two numbers were set once from the stored series' surge frequencies (volume only; 15.9%, 6.3% and 1.5% of days at ratios 1.5, 2 and 3, against 17.7%, 7.4% and 1.4% synthetic). Three new worlds pay a premium on the five candles after a surge day. Calibration of the family alone on 5,625 datasets (`CALIBRATION.md`, fifteen families): admitted in 1.0% and 1.2% of no-skill datasets; **only 1.5%, 2.8% and 6.2% with a premium of 0.5%, 1.0% and 2.0% per surge day (up to 6.7 times what a trade costs): the test has very little power for this family**, A3 on two years of VALIDATION being the limit (it failed in 85% to 95% of datasets), and A1 can fail (6.5% to 13.3% "too thin": the ratio-3 row has 12 surge days on TRAIN plus VALIDATION). So a rejection carries almost no information, which the note and the amendment say in their first lines. The fifteen-family no-skill bound is at most 16.8% and 22.4% (pooled, was 16.0% and 21.6%). Two things were counted on the real candles for the design and nothing else: the surge-day frequencies (volume only) and the number of surge days per period (149, 53 and 12 for the three ratios on TRAIN plus VALIDATION; volume and the sign of the day); no return after a surge day was computed before the run.
- **Engineering.** Tests first (hand-worked series at the boundaries: exactly twice the baseline counts, an equal close does not, the day is not in its own baseline, a zero baseline never fires, the exact fill rows and prices through the account engine for one surge and for two surges inside a hold, a plain-loop reference for the baseline, the no-look-ahead test over every configuration with a `walk()` fixture whose volume now changes from day to day, and a second definition in the novelty screen that must agree on every day of a 900-day walk for all nine settings); version 1.5; the old tests that name the version or the count now compare with the constants; view wording ("volume 2x its 20-day average") and logo VSG. The research run is `python -m atlas_api.btcquant.research` (two seconds).
- **Verified.** The whole backend suite (2,160 passed tests) after the run; 400 btcquant tests (378 before); the calibration on 5,625 synthetic datasets; a mutation pass of 50 deliberate breakages of the new code (baseline window and its offset, the comparison and its boundary, the rise, the zero guard, the hold and its extension, the stop, the explanation, the grid, the version pin, the logo and wording, the volume process and its random stream, and the surge world's window and premium): 49 caught, the survivor an equivalent change (`>=` against `>` on a continuous random volume), every file restored and its hash checked. **A correction caught on the way:** a first draft of the result said it was the closest any family had come to the admission bar; the reports of the first run show six families that failed A3 alone, so the sentence was removed (and a line added to the experiments skill). **Not verified:** that the daily run at 00:15 UTC on 2026-11-18 opens the `volume_surge-9c042d26` monitor (not run yet); the volume paper beyond its abstract (the title in `SOURCES.md` is from memory); any volume effect in regimes the source excludes, on other exchanges' volume, or after 2024; that the synthetic volume resembles real volume beyond its surge frequencies and persistence (the real link between volume and the size of a day's move is not modelled).
- **Credibly profitable?** No. A rejection of a volume rule that earned more than the matched hold after costs on 56 trades and still failed two gates, with a test that sees even a large premium about 6 times in 100; nothing here is evidence that any rule makes money. Status: not yet.
- **Next candidates.** BUILD_PLAN 14e: `squeeze_break` (the next by the fixed priority rule), but first the power point: do its calibration alone BEFORE deciding to pre-register it, and log "no change" with the numbers if it is not worth its N. 08 UTC is a data hour (housing starts).

## 2026-11-17 08:55 UTC (Claude, data job): US housing starts (Census Bureau and HUD via FRED), a new card on the Signal Macro tab, and a correction to the queue
- **Source added.** How many new homes American builders began building each month, from the Census Bureau and the Department of Housing and Urban Development through FRED: `HOUST` (total), `HOUST1F` (single-family) and `HOUST5F` (five or more units), seasonally adjusted annual rate in thousands of homes (`fred.stlouisfed.org/graph/fredgraph.csv?id=...`, free, no key, a plain client as for the other FRED cards). Checked live at 08:18 UTC: HTTP 200 on all three in 0.2 to 3.0 s, 813 monthly values each (1959-01 to 2026-09), none missing; the licence line on each series page is "Public Domain: Citation Requested" with no third-party notice (read 08:19).
- **What it shows (live, 2026-11-17).** September 2026: a pace of 1.221 million new homes a year, down 5.2% on August, 15.1% on three months ago and 7.4% on a year ago; single-family 859 thousand (70.4% of the total, up 2.6% on the year) and five or more units 349 thousand (28.6%, down 25.9% on the year); over ten years the change over a year ran from -26.4% (April 2020) to +59.1% (April 2021) and 18% of those months were weaker than now. The card says it is a yearly pace and not a count of the month's homes, and that the newest month is an estimate that is revised.
- **A correction found by reading the parts' notes, as the 05:3x queue note asked.** The queue called housing starts the next candidate "with an exact built-in check": total = single-family + two-to-four-unit + five-or-more-unit starts, true in all 813 months. It is true by construction. FRED's `HOUST2F` is calculated by Haver Analytics (a third party, which carries its own copyright notice) as the total minus the other two, so the sum proves nothing, and that series is not shipped (HELD in `DATA_SOURCES.md`). The retail sales identity is real (all three of its series are Census figures, notes read). A rule for it went into `DATA_LOOP.md` and the data-source checklist of the loop rules skill.
- **Checks built in, with limits set from the whole real history before any test.** Totals between 300 and 3,000 (real 478 in April 2009 to 2,494 in January 1972); no month moved outside -35% to +40% (real extremes -26.42% in March 1984 and +29.27% in July 1982); no yearly change outside -65% to +115% (real -54.80% in January 2009 and +96.19% in February 1983); single-family plus five-or-more never more than the total plus 1 (the real remainder is never below 3 since 1963); single-family 40% to 95% of the total (real 51.4% to 90.2%; swapped parts would give 21% to 36% in the last 24 months and are refused); the last 24 months in a row; a part missing or ending in another month refused; a failing check shows nothing. What the data can NOT check is stated in the module and on the card: there is no exact parts-add-up check.
- **Verified.** 27 new tests in `test_housingstarts.py` on the saved real answer (813 months) and made-up series: every figure worked by hand and again in plain Python before the module was written, every check at its boundary and one step over, each window's edge, the six real extremes of 1982 to 2021 that must not be refused, the swapped-parts case on the real data, the cache lifetimes for a success and a failure, the failure in words, demo, the route, the plain-client rule, and that `HOUST2F` is never asked for. **100 deliberate breakages: 96 caught and the four survivors are the exact -35%, +40%, -65% and +115% edges, which cannot be hit in floating point (the same kind as the three of the retail pass).** The whole backend suite (2,187 passed, 27 new), mobile `tsc` clean and 76 unit tests, the web export, and the full phone e2e 62 of 62 with every live card answering (so the new card's live content checks ran against the real figures); the screenshot of the card was read and its numbers match the backend's.
- **Not verified.** That the Census Bureau's estimates are right (the card says they are revised); the card on a real phone; that the October figure arrives on schedule (the release date is not in the app's calendar); the reasons behind any move (the card describes, it does not explain).
- **Credibly profitable?** Not applicable to this job (a data card describes homebuilding, not a signal). Status: not yet.
- **Next candidates.** Industrial production (`INDPRO`, Federal Reserve Board; a weighted aggregate with no identity: read its series notes first); the ECB's euro-area consumer prices (`ICP`) and money supply (`BSI`).

## 2026-11-17 10:55 UTC (Claude, strategy job; owner mission): btcquant step 14e, what the procedure can see in the three entries that are left: all three DEFERRED, nothing pre-registered or run
- **What was done: the rule first, the numbers second.** The 07:50 plan said to weigh the power of `squeeze_break` before pre-registering it, so a go/no-go rule was written into `BUILD_PLAN.md` 14e and pushed with the tool at 10:25 UTC (commit `7faf33d`), before any number of the study existed: an entry is pre-registered only if its default schedule (from the novelty screen, which reads prices and volume only; held fixed) is admitted by the five gates in at least 20% of 400 synthetic datasets in which a premium of 6.7 times what a trade costs (2.0% a trade) is planted on the days the schedule holds. The study ran from 10:26 to 10:33 UTC: the six queued schedules at five premiums (none, 1.7, 3.3, 6.7 and 13.3 times the cost) and 400 datasets each (seeds 1001 to 1400), then four entries at three larger premiums, added afterwards to describe how large a premium each needs and not part of the rule.
- **Result.** At 2.0% a trade: `squeeze_break` **8.5%** (6.1% to 11.6%; 21 trades in four years, only 7 of them on the two VALIDATION years, and the timing test A3 failed in 88% of datasets), `money_flow` 8.8%, `near_high` 15.0%, against 20.0% for `volume_surge`, 83.0% for `three_down` and 98.5% for `monday`. **All three entries that are left are below the bar, so all three are DEFERRED, not rejected: nothing pre-registered or run, N stays 113, no family or gate changed, the queue order unchanged.** To be found in about half the datasets they need roughly 10% a trade (`squeeze_break`), 6% (`money_flow`) and 4% (`near_high`). What decides the power is the number of trades, not the number of candles: the rules that hold a position for five days or more make 21 to 87 trades in four years.
- **The method's check and its limits.** For the two entries that already have a full-procedure calibration, the schedule-level number is 2.2 to 3.2 times the full procedure's (20.0% against 6.2% for `volume_surge`, 83.0% against 38.3% for `three_down`), so every number is a best case, with no selection step and no neighbour test. The bar is not high either: `volume_surge` sat exactly on it. The limits are written next to the table in `CALIBRATION.md` ("What the procedure can see in the entries that are left").
- **The owner's choice** (`BUILD_PLAN.md` 14e and `RESEARCH_QUEUE.md`): lower the bar and run the three anyway (85% to 92% would be "rejected" even if a premium of 2.0% a trade were there; N goes from 113 to 143), add data that gives the tests more trades (other assets, finer candles or an earlier start: a protocol amendment with its own calibration; my recommendation, starting with the read-only look at what is free, 14f), or stop the queue. Saying "run it anyway" overrides my rule.
- **A correction caught on the way.** The plan text said the rule was fixed "at about 10:35 UTC"; the commit is dated 10:25:16 and the first output of the study 10:26. Corrected in `BUILD_PLAN.md`, and a line added to the experiments skill about writing a time into a document that claims "before".
- **Engineering.** `calibration.py`: `candles_from_returns`, `held_candles`, `schedule_returns` (the mild null world's returns plus the premium on held candles), `break_even_per_held_day`, `fixed_schedule` (a temporary one-setting stop-based family, removed in a `finally`), `run_schedule_world` and the command `calibration schedule <entry> <multiple> <first seed> <end seed> [out.json]`. A schedule with no premium gives exactly the dataset the mild null world gives for that seed (test).
- **Verified.** 409 btcquant tests (400 before; nine new: the tool's seven pushed at 10:25, one equality test, one command test) and the whole backend suite (2,196 passed, none failed); a mutation pass of 38 deliberate breakages of the new code (the premium's sign, size and place; the held-candle shift; the cost and the trade and day counts; the family's kind, state and stop; the trial count; the order of the periods; the seeds; the record; the command): 34 caught at first and 4 survived: two range-edge cases of the break-even (entries against exits; ON candles against held candles), the command's premium taken from TRAIN alone (its test used `monday`, whose cost per day held is 0.30% in any range) and the printed count of admitted datasets (never read by the test). Two assertions and one test were added, all four are caught on the rerun, the file was restored and its hash checked after both runs.
- **Not verified.** That the schedule-level numbers carry over to the other entries' full procedure (the factor is known for two entries only); anything about real returns (none was computed); settings other than the default of each rule; real Bitcoin's fat tails, clustering volatility and drift (the worlds are independent normal returns with a premium that is the same on every held day); whether finer candles, an earlier start or other coins are available free and clean (14f); that the daily run at 00:15 UTC on 2026-11-18 opens the `three_down-e7923e48` and `volume_surge-9c042d26` monitors (not run yet).
- **Credibly profitable?** No. This job tested the test, not a strategy: four years of daily candles cannot tell a premium of 2.0% a trade from chance for four of the six queued rules, and nothing here says that any rule makes money. Status: not yet.
- **Next candidates.** 11 UTC data: industrial production (`INDPRO`; read its series notes first) or the ECB's euro-area consumer prices and money supply. 12 UTC improve. 13 UTC strategy: BUILD_PLAN 14f (a read-only look at free data options), then "no change" unless the owner has chosen a way out of 14e.

## 2026-11-17 11:45 UTC (Claude, data job): US industrial production and capacity utilization (Federal Reserve Board via FRED), a new card on the Signal Macro tab, and a check the queue said did not exist
- **Source added.** How much US factories, mines and electric and gas utilities produced each month, and how much of their capacity was in use, from the Board of Governors of the Federal Reserve System (release G.17) through FRED: `INDPRO` (total index), `IPMANSICS` (manufacturing, SIC basis), `IPMINE` (mining), `IPUTIL` (electric and gas utilities) and `TCU` (capacity utilization), seasonally adjusted, index 2017 = 100 (`fred.stlouisfed.org/graph/fredgraph.csv?id=...`, free, no key, a plain client as for the other FRED cards). Checked live at 11:2x to 11:3x UTC: HTTP 200 on all five in 0.2 to 1.1 s, 1,293 monthly values for the total from 1919-01 to 2026-09. The series notes of all five were read first, as the 08:55 lesson asks: "Public Domain: Citation Requested" on each page, no third-party copyright notice, nothing derived from the others by a third party. The INDPRO page said "Updated: Oct 16, 2026" and "Next Release Date: Nov 17, 2026" (the day of this cycle).
- **What it shows (live, 2026-11-17).** September 2026: the index at 103.95 (2017 = 100), up 0.7% on August, 1.0% on three months ago and 2.2% on a year ago; manufacturing 98.44 (up 1.1% on the year), mining 124.52 (up 2.8%) and electric and gas utilities 117.25 (up 8.7%); capacity in use 76.9% (up 0.8 points on a year ago). Over ten years the change over a year ran from -17.3% (April 2020) to +16.6% (April 2021) and 76% of those months were weaker than now. The card says it is the volume produced and not dollars, that the newest month is an estimate that is revised, and that it is not a signal or a forecast.
- **A check the queue said did not exist.** The queue called industrial production "a weighted aggregate with no identity to check against". It has no exact identity, but it has a property that must hold: the total's one-month change is a weighted average of its three groups' changes, so it lies between the lowest and the highest of them. On the whole history it holds in all 665 months from May 1971 to September 2026 with the SIC manufacturing series (`IPMANSICS`), and fails in four months since 1972 (by up to 0.059 points) with the NAICS one (`IPMAN`), which is how the right series was chosen; 76 months before May 1971 break it (the worst 0.66 points in November 1939), so it is applied to the last 24 months only, with a tolerance of 0.02 points. The second check is weaker and says so: the capacity implied by output and utilization (output divided by utilization) moves between -0.5% and +1.0% a month (real -0.21% to +0.67% since 1967); utilization is output divided by capacity by definition, so it shows that the two series agree, not that either is right. The other limits were set from the whole real history before any test (the total 3.69 to 104.10; one-month change -13.19% in April 2020 to +16.55% in May 1933; a group -41.96% to +31.00%, both mining in 1922; year on year -33.66% in February 1946 to +62.04% in July 1933).
- **Also checked live, not shipped (queue note in `DATA_SOURCES.md`).** The ECB's euro-area money supply M3 and its published growth are current (17,717,267 million euro and 3.504% for 2026-09, provisional), but the euro-area consumer prices `ICP/M.U2.N.000000.4.ANR` and `.INX` end at 2025-12 (an observation comment begins "As of 4 February 2026 onwards, the ...", not read in full), so prices need a successor key first.
- **Verified.** 35 new tests in `test_industrialproduction.py` on the saved real answer (177 months of each of five series): every figure worked by hand and again in plain Python outside the module, every check at its edges one at a time on synthetic series that satisfy the others by construction, each check's 24-month window, the order of the checks, 154 real months (2013-12 to 2026-09) that must never be refused and the real shocks of 2020 and 2021 read as they were, the failure in words, the two cache lifetimes, demo, the route, the plain-client rule. A breakage run of 98 deliberate changes: 82 caught at first and 13 survived; six of those were real gaps and are closed (the exact edges of the group move limit; the year being exactly twelve months, which my first tests could not tell from eleven or thirteen because the series was flat around it; the one-month change in utilization, which rounded to the same figure against two months back; and a redundant floor at zero in the between-groups check, removed); seven are comparisons at an exact limit (`<=` against `<` at the total's monthly move, the yearly change and the capacity move, and `>` against `>=` at the tolerance) that no input can tell apart in floating point (140 against 100 gives +39.99999999999999%, 70 against 100 gives -30.000000000000004%); the rerun caught 91 of 98 and the file was restored and its hash checked after each run. The whole backend suite (2,231 passed, none failed); phone: typecheck, 76 unit tests, web export, and the browser tests 62 of 62 with the live card (every live card had data, so every content check ran, the accessibility gate included); the screenshot was read: the card sits after the housing starts card, light TradingView-style look, no clipping.
- **Not verified.** That the October figure (due 2026-11-17) passes the checks when it arrives (the 24-month window moves one month; the between-groups property was verified through September); that the Board's own estimate is right (the card says it is revised); the card on a real phone; the release date, which comes from the FRED page and not from the Board; the reasons behind any move.
- **Credibly profitable?** Not applicable to this job (a data card describes industrial output, not a signal). Status: not yet.
- **Next candidates.** The ECB's euro-area money supply M3 with its published growth and, for a real check, M1 and M2 (M1 <= M2 <= M3 always; the published growth is built from adjusted flows, so its gap to the growth of the stocks is to be measured first), or the successor key for euro-area prices. 12 UTC improve; 13 UTC strategy: BUILD_PLAN 14f (a read-only look at free data options), then "no change" unless the owner has chosen a way out of 14e.

## 2026-11-17 13:30 UTC (Claude, strategy job; owner mission): btcquant step 14f, what extra candle data would give the tests more trades: not enough, and the recommendation changed to stopping the daily queue
- **What was done.** The read-only look that 14e asked for, written up in `btcquant/DATA_OPTIONS.md`. Candles were downloaded from Coinbase's public endpoint (free, no key, the plain client of `data.py`) into a scratch folder, 141 requests in about 31 seconds, none rate-limited, and checked with the module's own structural checks. Nothing under `btcquant/data` changed, no protocol text changed, no family was run and no strategy return was computed. Ethereum and Solana were fetched only to 2024-12-31 so that the TEST period stays unseen for them.
- **What exists.** Bitcoin daily from 2015-07-20 (the earliest the endpoint serves): 1,261 candles before the stored series, none missing, joining the stored file without a jump (3,691.86 then 3,691.87), 5 requests. Ethereum daily from 2016-05-18: 3,148 of 3,150 candles to 2024-12-31 (one gap of two candles), 11 requests. Solana daily from 2021-06-17: 1,294 of 1,294, 5 requests. Bitcoin hourly from 2015-07-20: 30,207 of 30,243 candles to 2018-12-31, 36 missing in 12 gaps (the longest 15 hours), 120 requests. The hourly file from 2019 on was already stored (68,990 candles, 9 gaps of 1 to 3 hours; the daily run does not refresh it). No structural problem in any of it; the only flags are two real crashes (Ethereum -43.3% on 2020-03-12, Solana -42.6% on 2022-11-09).
- **What it gives the three rules left.** Entries of their default schedules after a year of warm-up, to 2024-12-31: `squeeze_break` 21 trades in the research data now and 64 if every dataset were added up (8 more from early Bitcoin, 25 from Ethereum, 10 from Solana), `money_flow` 87 and 324, `near_high` 69 and 209. The extra trades are far from independent: daily return correlation with Bitcoin 0.835 for Ethereum (2020 to 2024) and 0.681 for Solana; 29% of Bitcoin's `squeeze_break` entries have an Ethereum entry within three days, 59% for `money_flow`. By a rough square-root rule (not measured) a premium of 2.0% a trade needs about 1,200, 1,800 and 600 trades; the three stay 3 to 19 times short even if all of it counted, and the premium found 8 times in 10 would only fall to about 8.6%, 4.8% and 3.4%. A second limit is untouched: gate A3 judges VALIDATION alone, two years, in which `squeeze_break` makes 7 trades.
- **Finer candles are the only option that multiplies the trades,** and they are already stored: "three lower closes in a row" fires 2,480 times on hourly bars in 2020 to 2024 against 98 times on daily bars. But the median hourly close-to-close move was 0.255% (daily 1.50%), below the 0.30% a round trip costs, and 44% of hours moved more than 0.30%: a rule on hourly bars must earn several times a typical hour's move on every trade, and it needs new families, an hourly version of the synthetic worlds and the power tool (both daily today) and a protocol amendment.
- **The recommendation of 14e changed.** At 14e I recommended adding data. The numbers say more daily data is not enough, so the recommendation is now (3) stop the daily queue (the fifteen families stay the record; the daily run keeps paper-trading the chosen configurations; the TEST period stays sealed), with (2b) hourly candles as the direction only if the owner wants the search to go on (first an hourly power tool, my estimate about three strategy hours). The owner decides; "run it anyway" still overrides my rule.
- **Corrections made before publishing.** My first draft of the document said the pooled data would let `near_high` and `volume_surge` be tested for premiums of 5% to 6% (those were today's premiums; pooled they are 3.4% and 2.9%), that a year of waiting is worth more than any option (not supported: a year adds about 4 `squeeze_break` trades), and gave a request rate of 8 a second (measured 4.5). All three were fixed against the saved numbers.
- **Verified.** Every figure above was recomputed from the saved downloads and the stored files with the module's own `validate` and the novelty screen's schedule functions; the 409 btcquant tests pass (only documents changed); `git status` shows nothing under `btcquant/data`.
- **Not verified.** The square-root rule (the power study measured 21 to 261 trades only); that the extra candles are right (one exchange, no second source: Binance, Bitstamp, Kraken and Bitfinex refuse this machine; the early market was thin); the independence of the extra assets' trades beyond the correlations and overlaps given; any rule's returns on any of this data (none computed).
- **Credibly profitable?** No. Nothing was tested for an edge in this job; it measured what the tests can see. Status: not yet.
- **Next candidates.** Strategy hours log "no change" until the owner chooses a way out of 14e (`DATA_OPTIONS.md` has the numbers). 14 UTC data hour: the ECB's euro-area M3.

## 2026-11-17 14:49 UTC (Claude, data job): euro-area money supply (M1, M2, M3 from the ECB Data Portal), a new card on the Signal Macro tab
- **Source added.** How much money the euro area holds and how fast it grows, from the European Central Bank's Data Portal API (`data-api.ecb.europa.eu`, the BSI dataset: eleven monthly stock series for M1, M2, M3 and their parts, and the ECB's own annual growth rates for M1, M2 and M3; free, no key). Checked live at 14:1x to 14:4x UTC: HTTP 200 on both requests in 3 to 11 s, 561 monthly values from 1980-01 for M1, M2 and M3 and 349 from 1997-09 for the parts, none missing. Every observation carries the flag F (free). The portal's own pages are not reachable from this machine (only the API host is), so their terms page could not be read; the earlier ECB cards were shipped on the same basis, naming the ECB and its Data Portal.
- **What it shows (live, 2026-11-17).** At the end of September 2026 (provisional) M3 was €17.72 trillion, growing 3.5% a year (the ECB's own rate); M2 €16.49 trillion (3.3%) and M1 €11.33 trillion (2.7%); M3 is 64.0% currency and overnight deposits, 14.6% deposits with an agreed maturity of up to two years, 14.5% deposits redeemable at notice and 6.9% repurchase agreements, money market fund shares and short debt securities. Over ten years the ECB's rate ran from -1.3% (August 2023) to +12.5% (January 2021); 31% of those months were slower than now.
- **What the checks are, and what they are not.** All four sums (M1 = currency + overnight deposits; M2 = M1 + the two other deposit kinds; marketable instruments = repos + money market funds + short debt; M3 = M2 + those) hold to the million euro in every one of the 561 (and 349) months, because the ECB builds the aggregates from the parts: they show that the series fetched belong to one release, not that the figures are right, and the card says so. The independent check is the ECB's published growth against the growth of the amounts over twelve months: within 4 points for M3 (4.5 for M2, 7 for M1), limits set from the whole history (the real maxima are 3.76, 4.10 and 6.41 points; in the last 24 months M3's gap ran from -0.24 to +0.78 points, the largest in September 2026; the large gaps of the past fall in the years countries joined the euro area, so it is loose by necessity). A new kind of check: the provider refuses a series whose unit labels changed (the ECB labels euro in millions and percent change), which catches a unit slip that a range check can miss.
- **Also found.** The euro-area consumer prices did not need a successor key: the whole `ICP` dataflow ends at 2025-12 for every area, and the figures moved to a new `HICP` dataflow (euro-area total `HICP.M.U2.N.000000.4D0.ANR`, 4.0 for 2026-10 as an estimate); queued in `DATA_SOURCES.md`. **A flake fixed:** the browser test for the miner revenue card expected four decimals of transaction fees and failed whenever the fourth digit was a zero ("2.571 BTC"); the card now always shows four ("2.5710 BTC").
- **Found by the tests.** Comparing a rounded maximum with an unrounded candidate let the later of two equal gaps win, so the month named for the largest gap was the wrong one on a tie; the largest gap is now kept unrounded and rounded only for output.
- **Verified.** 36 new tests in `test_euromoney.py` on the saved real answers (177 months of fourteen series): every figure worked by hand and again in plain Python outside the module, every check at its edges one at a time on synthetic worlds that satisfy the others by construction, each check's 24-month window, 141 real months since 2015 that must never be refused and the real shocks of 2020, 2021 and 2023 read as they were, the plus signs of the key reaching the ECB unchanged, the failure in words, the cache lifetimes, demo, the route. A breakage run of 118 deliberate changes: 106 caught at first and 12 survived; ten were real gaps and are closed (the ceilings of M1 and M2, the exact address of the card's link, units padded with spaces, the edges of the growth range and of the gap tolerance, the rounding of levels in the summary and the history, which request asks for how many observations); two are comparisons at an exact limit of the monthly move that no input can reach in floating point (0.95 against 1 gives -5.000000000000004%); the rerun caught 116 of 118 and the file was restored and its hash checked. Phone: typecheck, 78 unit tests, web export, browser tests 62 of 62 with the live cards (35 card source links measured on the Macro screen, the smallest 45 points; no tap target under 44 by 44), the screenshot read: the card sits after the ECB interest rates card in the same light style. The whole backend suite (2,267 passed, none failed).
- **Not verified.** The portal's terms (host not reachable); that the November figure (due about 2026-11-27) passes the checks when it arrives; why M3's gap against the published growth is widest now (not looked into); that the ECB's own figures are right (the card says so); the card on a real phone.
- **Credibly profitable?** Not applicable to this job (a data card describes money in the euro area, not a signal). Status: not yet.
- **Next candidates.** Euro-area consumer prices from the new `HICP` dataflow (read its notes and status codes first; the annual rate is calculated from the index, so the pair cannot check each other), or the ECB's bank lending (loans to households and firms; look for an identity in the dataset's own items before relying on it). 15 UTC skills; 16 UTC strategy: "no change" with a pointer to `btcquant/DATA_OPTIONS.md` unless the owner has chosen a way out of 14e.

- **One thing worth knowing.** The rehearsal placed an entry order for `volume_surge-9c042d26` at once. That is the rule, not an error: a surge day (volume at least 1.5 times the mean of the 20 days before it, close above the previous close) keeps the position on for ten candles counting itself, the real candles contain one on 2026-11-15 (volume 15,514 against a mean of 8,747, ratio 1.77, close up; earlier ones on 11-04 and 11-09), so the state stays on through the 2026-11-24 candle, and a new account starts in whatever state its rule is in on its first bar (as the four accounts that bought at the 2026-11-15 open did). But the order's `info` in the ledger holds the numbers of the signal bar (a ratio of 1.42 against the 1.5 required, in the rehearsal), not the surge day that explains the order, so the ledger line reads as a contradiction. The phone does not use `info` (its wording of a buy reads the reference close, the stop and the estimated size, `view.py`). Not changed: the explanation is code of a family under the protocol, and neither the state, the stop nor the order depends on it; `DAILY_LOOP.md` now says how to read it and how to repeat the rehearsal. An option for later, if the owner wants the ledger self-explanatory: make the family's explanation name the surge day.

The daily trigger (`trig_01WtA5eVtrtDZWdoqvfNpQXd`): enabled, next run 2026-11-18 00:15 UTC. It fires into this same session, so a stalled session delays it: the 2026-11-16 and 2026-11-17 fires were both delivered at 04:17 UTC on 2026-11-17 (the outage logged on that date) and one run caught up two candles by design.

## 2026-11-17 18:02 UTC (Claude, data job): euro-area consumer prices (HICP, compiled by Eurostat, from the ECB Data Portal), a new card on the Signal Macro tab
- **Source added.** How fast prices are rising in the euro area and which parts of the basket drive it, from the ECB Data Portal API (`data-api.ecb.europa.eu`, the HICP dataflow that replaced the old `ICP` one in 2026: annual rates of seven series, indices of six and yearly basket weights of six; free, no key). Checked live at 17:2x to 17:5x UTC: HTTP 200 on all three production requests in 7 to 10 s (one request for seven series of 121 months stalled for 60 s once and answered in 8.6 s on the retry; a wildcard over every item timed out at 60 s, so the keys list the items). The series attributes were read first: units percent change, index points and per mille, index base 2025 = 100, provider 4D0 (Eurostat), status E on the newest month, no third-party notice.
- **What it shows (live, 2026-11-17).** October 2026, an early (flash) estimate: consumer prices 4.0% above a year earlier (3.8% in September, 2.9% in July), core (without energy, food, alcohol and tobacco) 2.5%. Energy is 9.0% of the basket and +20.8% a year, about 1.9 points of the 4.0; services 46.8% at +3.3% (1.6 points); non-energy industrial goods 25.2% at +1.2% (0.3); unprocessed food 5.1% at +4.6% (0.2); processed food with alcohol and tobacco 13.8% at +0.5% (0.1); 79% of the last 120 months were below 4.0 (range -0.3% in September 2020 to +10.6% in October 2022).
- **What the checks are, and what they are not.** Limits were set from the whole record before any test was written (358 months from 1997, 311 for the two parts that start in December 2000). **Not Eurostat's own sums:** the headline lies between the lowest and the highest rate of its five parts and core lies between goods and services, in all 311 real months with no tolerance (chain-linking could in principle break it; it does not). **Eurostat's own arithmetic, said so on the card:** the all-items index equals the previous December's index times the weighted average of the parts' indices relative to their own December with the year's weights (gap at most 0.021 index points in 310 months since 2001, 0.009 in the last 24), and each published rate matches the rate its index gives (at most 0.050 points in all 2,054 month-series pairs): the second one is derived (the source calculates one from the other), which corrects the 14:4x note that feared it. The points of the headline are share times rate, an approximation (within 0.1 points in 292 of 310 real months, 0.51 at most in March 2022 with energy +44.3%): shown only when the sum is within 0.3 of the headline (hidden in 5 real months).
- **Found while building it.** The queue note was half right: the new flow is current to 2026-10 but `IGOODS` (industrial goods) is a month behind the rest and `FOODAT`, `NEIG00` and `GD0000` are not returned, so the card uses the five parts that partition the basket (weights add up to 1000 in every year since 2001). A synthetic test world has to start in a December: the all-items index is chained from the December before.
- **Found by the breakage run.** 204 deliberate one-line changes to the module: 142 caught at first, 62 not. **46 of the 62 were limits changed in the module while my tests read the limit back from the module** (test and code moved together): the tests now spell the limits out and one test pins the constants (loop-rules item 15). Ten were real gaps and are closed: a row of another item carrying that item's own key, the December base when no checked month is a year after it, the index range checked in the oldest months, equal and negative index gaps, and five rounding places that showed up only with unrounded rates. **Six remain and cannot be killed in floating point:** a comparison changed from `<=` to `<` exactly at a tolerance (the one-month moves at their bounds, the weight sum, the rate gap, the identity, the contribution tolerance); the tests probe 0.01 inside and outside each.
- **Verified.** 40 new tests in `test_eurohicp.py` on the saved real answers (178 months of seven series, indices of six and the weights): every figure worked by hand and again in plain Python outside the module, every check at its edges one at a time on synthetic worlds that satisfy the others by construction, the 24-month windows, 142 real months since 2015 that must never be refused and the real shocks of 2020, 2022 and 2023 read as they were; whole backend suite 2,307 passed; phone typecheck, 78 unit tests, `export:web`, browser tests 62 of 62 with every live card present (the new card's sentences, five parts with shares adding up to 100 within 0.3, the checks and the note matched against the live answer); the top and the bottom of the card read in real screenshots.
- **Not verified.** The portal's terms (the host is not reachable from here; Eurostat's re-use policy, free re-use with the source acknowledged, is from memory and was not re-read); that the final October figure (due about 2026-11-18, status A) passes the checks when it replaces the estimate; why the points approximation widens when prices move a lot (a property of chain-linking, not looked into further); the card on a real phone and with a real screen reader.
- **Credibly profitable?** Not applicable to this job (a data card describes prices in the euro area, not a signal). Status: not yet.
- **Next candidates.** The ECB's bank lending (BSI loans to households and firms; look for an exact identity in the dataset's own items), or, not yet checked live: the ECB's composite indicator of systemic stress (`CISS`, daily), the euro-area yield curve (`YC`) and euro-area unemployment. 18 UTC improve; 19 UTC strategy: "no change" with the pointer to `btcquant/DATA_OPTIONS.md` until the owner chooses.

## 2026-11-17 20:50 UTC (Claude, data job): euro-area unemployment (Eurostat's labour force survey, from the ECB Data Portal), a new card on the Signal Macro tab
- **First checked, not shipped.** Hyperliquid's public info endpoint (`POST api.hyperliquid.xyz/info`, no key) answers from this machine (HTTP 200 in 0.4 s, and 0.5 s when repeated at 20:53 UTC: funding, open interest, mark and oracle prices of 235 perpetual markets), unlike the other derivatives venues; the register already says it is in the BTC derivatives panel (cycle 9), so nothing was added. The queue's next candidate was taken.
- **Source added.** How many people in the euro area are out of work, by sex and by age, from the ECB Data Portal API (`data-api.ecb.europa.eu`, the LFSI dataflow: the rate and the number of unemployed persons of nine series each; free, no key). Checked live from this machine: HTTP 200 in 2 to 4 s per request while building it, and the module's own two requests returned the same September 2026 figures in 1.1 s when repeated at about 20:55 UTC; 321 monthly values per series from 2000-01 to 2026-09, none missing, every status normal ("A"). The series title says what it is: "Euro area 20 (fixed composition) as of 1 January 2023", seasonally adjusted, unlike the changing composition of the prices card, and the card says so.
- **What it shows (live, 2026-11-17).** September 2026: 6.6% of the labour force unemployed (6.5% in August and June, 6.4% a year earlier), 11.45 million people (427 thousand more than a year ago); women 6.8%, men 6.4%, under 25 15.4%, 25 to 74 5.7%; 49% of the unemployed are women and 21% are under 25 (calculated from the published numbers, and said so); over ten years the rate ran from 6.2% (October 2024) to 9.9% (October 2016) and 28% of the months were lower.
- **What the checks are, and what they are not.** Limits were set from the whole record before any test was written. **Not Eurostat's own sums:** the rate for everybody lies between the rates of women and men and between those of the under 25 and the 25 to 74, in all 321 real months with no tolerance. **Eurostat's own arithmetic, said so on the card:** the persons of women and men add up to everybody's and the under 25 and the 25 to 74 add up to everybody's (to within 0.1 thousand, the rounding, in all 321 months: the levels are additive, so these hold by construction and prove only that the series belong to one release), and the labour force implied by the parts (persons over rate) agrees with the one implied for everybody within 1.37% for the sexes and 1.38% for the ages in every month (the rounding of a one-decimal rate; tolerance 2.5%).
- **Found while building it.** The persons and the rates of one release are consistent through an unpublished labour force, so a mix-up of two series shows as a labour-force gap larger than the rounding explains; a test world had to be built from women, men and the under 25 (everything else follows), and the edges of the age check needed a world where the under 25 are close to everybody so that a made-up rate stays inside the move bound.
- **Found by the breakage run.** 190 deliberate one-line changes to the module: 176 caught at first, 14 not. Ten were real gaps and are closed: two range-edge comparisons were missed because the assertion looked for "%, outside" while the message read "% in 2026-09, outside" (a negative assertion that could never fail), two rate-move edges now have a test with numbers that are exact in binary (a flat 10.0 and a step of exactly the bound), three months-back changes and two rounding changes survived because the first world's drift rounded to the same numbers (the world now drifts by 0.1237 and 0.0731 points a month and the test asserts that its figures differ between one and two decimals and that every span gives a different change), and one value that was kept but never used (the month of the largest sum gap) was removed. **Four remain and cannot be killed in floating point:** the one-month bounds of the persons (-10% and +15%: a search found no pair of numbers giving exactly those percentages), the sum tolerance and the labour-force tolerance. Two lessons added to `atlas-loop-rules` item 15.
- **Verified.** 34 new tests in `test_eurounemployment.py` on the saved real answers (the five series used and two the parser must ignore, 153 months from 2014): every figure worked by hand and again in plain Python outside the module, every check at its edges one at a time on synthetic worlds that satisfy the others by construction, the 24-month windows, 141 real months since 2015 that must never be refused and the real shocks of 2015, March, April and July 2020; whole backend suite 2,350 passed; phone typecheck, 78 unit tests, `export:web`, browser tests 62 of 62 with every live card present (twice: a plus sign on the ten-year range of a rate was removed after the first screenshot), the top and the bottom of the card read in real screenshots. The check figures above (no violation of either between-check in 321 months, sum gaps of 0.1 thousand, labour-force gaps of 1.366% for the sexes in December 2025 and 1.384% for the ages in April 2024) were recomputed from the raw downloads outside the module at about 20:55 UTC.
- **Not verified.** The portal's terms (the host is not reachable from here; Eurostat's re-use policy is from memory); that the October figure (due about the start of December) passes the checks when it arrives; why the labour force implied by the sexes is furthest from the total's in December 2025 (the rounding explains up to about 1.5%, not looked into further); the card on a real phone and with a real screen reader.
- **Credibly profitable?** Not applicable to this job (a data card describes the euro-area labour market, not a signal). Status: not yet.
- **Next candidates.** The ECB's bank lending (BSI loans to households and firms; look for an exact identity in the dataset's own items), or, not yet checked live: `CISS` (daily systemic stress), the euro-area yield curve (`YC`) and euro-area GDP (`MNA`). 21 UTC skills; 22 UTC strategy: "no change" until the owner chooses (`btcquant/DATA_OPTIONS.md`); 00 UTC improve: the Journey feed wording noted in `LOOP.md`.

## 2026-11-17 23:53 UTC (Claude, data job): euro-area financial stress (the ECB's new Composite Indicator of Systemic Stress, daily), a new card on the Signal Macro tab
- **Chosen after reading, not built from memory.** The 21 UTC probe had named `CISS` and `YC` with keys written from memory. One request for the latest value of every series of the CISS dataflow (60 series, 3 s) showed (a) the old stress index ends on 2025-05-02 while the new one (`SS_CIN`) is current, (b) the new index comes with six contribution series (five market segments and the correlation between them) that add up to it, and (c) country-level and sovereign-stress series exist (not used). That made CISS the better candidate than `YC` (no identity known) and gave it a real check.
- **Source added.** The ECB's New Composite Indicator of Systemic Stress for the euro area (changing composition), daily, from the ECB Data Portal API (`data-api.ecb.europa.eu`, CISS dataflow; free, no key): the index and six contributions. Checked live 23:19 to 23:31 UTC: HTTP 200 in 3 s and 5 s for the whole history, 9 s for the module's own two requests (2,700 days of the index, 520 days of the contributions); 12,236 daily values of the index from 1980-01-03 and 7,272 of each contribution from 1999-01-01, none missing, all status A.
- **What it shows (live, 2026-11-17, newest day 2026-11-16).** Index 0.0054 on a 0-to-1 scale (higher is more stress); 0.0059 a week back and 0.0096 a month back; of the 2,616 days of the past ten years 8% were calmer, the median was 0.0437, the lowest 0.0007 (2017-11-27) and the highest 0.7380 (2022-10-17); made of bond market 0.0310, financial intermediaries 0.0269, equity market 0.0180, money market 0.0129 and foreign exchange 0.0079 (total 0.0966) and a correlation term of -0.0912.
- **What the checks are and are not.** Limits were set from the whole record before any test. Plain checks: units (the ECB's labels: a pure number), all series end on the same day, no hole of more than 5 days (real gaps 1 to 3), the index inside 0 to 1 (real 0.0007 to 0.9409) and moving by at most 0.25 a day (real +0.1364 on 2010-08-11 and -0.1051 on 1981-10-22). **Properties that are not the ECB's own sum:** each segment contributes between 0 and 1 (never negative in 7,272 days, real range 0.0005 to 0.2569) and the correlation term lies between -1 and 0 (never positive, real -0.4103 to -0.0010). **The ECB's own arithmetic, said so on the card:** the six contributions add up to the index (at most 3.3e-16 apart in all 7,272 days, tolerance 0.000001): it shows that the seven series belong to one release, not that the figures are right. A value the source leaves empty with status M is left out, never filled.
- **Found while building it.** The old and the new series sit side by side (a key from memory could have fetched the stale one without any error: loop-rules item 16). The index exceeds its largest single contribution on 54% of days (3,932 of 7,272), so that is NOT a property and was not used. A weekday-only synthetic world has 2,608 days in the ten-year window where the real series has 2,616 (the test pins the count it computed by hand). A decimal that sits halfway (0.02345) rounds to 0.0234 in binary, so a hand-derived test literal was wrong (a value that is not halfway replaced it).
- **Found by the breakage run.** 178 deliberate one-line changes: 167 caught at first, 11 not. Two of the 11 were code that did nothing and were removed (a count of the dots in a key that the exact key comparison already covered, and an upper bound on a window whose last day is its newest); the other nine exposed eight gaps in the tests, all closed: a three-digit day passing the day pattern, spaces around a status, a largest move that was the largest rise instead of the largest in size, a gap exactly at the tolerance (testable here: every contribution 0 and an index of exactly the number 0.000001), a highest value rounded to three decimals (the fixture value needed a fourth decimal), 29 and 31 days back (a Monday-ending world has the same Friday for all three, so a Wednesday-ending one was added), the units of a real response for the index and for a contribution, and a demo message asserted by one word only. Second run: **178 of 178 caught, none unkillable** (earlier providers had two to six equality-at-a-bound changes that floating point could not reach; here the exact-in-binary edges 0.25, 1.0, -1.0 and 0.0 and a world with a gap exactly equal to the tolerance reached them).
- **Verified.** 35 new tests in `test_eurostress.py` on a real fixture (the index for 2,700 days and the six contributions for 520, rounded to 8 decimals, and the raw layout of a real response): every figure worked by hand and in plain Python outside the module, every check on both sides of its limit on synthetic worlds where the other checks hold by construction, the order of the checks, the reach of each check (whole window or the last 520 days), the real window never refused and its largest real move pinned, the units of real responses. Whole backend suite 2,385 passed; phone typecheck, 78 unit tests, `export:web`, browser tests 62 of 62 with every live card present (twice, the second after adding a bottom-of-card screenshot); the Macro accessibility probe measured 38 card source links (the smallest 45 points) with no violation; the top and the bottom of the card read in real screenshots; the module's live run gave the same figures as the fixture.
- **Not verified.** The ECB's terms (the website host is refused here; its re-use policy is from memory); how the ECB builds the index or why its correlation term is always negative (the card says only that it has been, and that the contributions add up); that a one-day move of more than 0.25 would be shown (it would hide the card by design: the bound is 1.8 times the largest real move); the card on a real phone and with a real screen reader.
- **Credibly profitable?** Not applicable to this job (a data card describes stress that already happened, not a signal). Status: not yet.
- **Next candidates.** The euro-area yield curve (`YC`: list the dataflow's series first, look for an identity, read its notes), ECB bank lending (`BSI` loans), euro-area GDP (`ESTAT:MNA`), or the sovereign-stress series of this same dataflow. 00 UTC improve: the Journey feed wording in `LOOP.md`; after the 00:15 UTC daily run check `dashboard.json` `opened_today`.

## 2026-11-18 02:49 UTC (Claude, data job): euro-area government bond yields (the ECB's yield curve for AAA-rated government bonds, daily), a new card on the Signal Macro tab
- **Chosen after reading, not built from memory.** The 23:53 note had put `YC` first. One request for the latest value of every series of the AAA curve (1,083 series, all current to 2026-11-16, all status A: 358 spot rates `SR_`, 358 par yields `PY_`, 358 instantaneous forward rates `IF_`, the three spreads and the six model parameters) and then the whole record of the fifteen series the card uses (5,675 business days from 2004-09-06, none missing, all status A) were read before any limit was fixed. The par yields and forward rates were not used (other quantities than the spot rates, no identity tested against them).
- **Source added.** The ECB's yield curve for AAA-rated euro-area government bonds (Svensson model, continuous compounding), daily, from the ECB Data Portal API (`data-api.ecb.europa.eu`, YC dataflow; free, no key): the 3-month, 1, 2, 5, 10 and 30-year spot rates, the three spreads of the 10-year rate over the 3-month, 1-year and 2-year rates, and the six model parameters of the newest day. Checked live 02:43 UTC: HTTP 200 for the three requests in 1.0 s, 0.2 s and 0.1 s (3.4 s for the module's overview; 7.8 s for the same three requests earlier in the hour).
- **What it shows (live, 2026-11-18, newest day 2026-11-16).** Spot rates 2.65% for 3 months, 2.99% for 1 year, 3.11% for 2 years, 3.17% for 5 years, 3.42% for 10 years and 3.67% for 30 years; against a week back +0.04, +0.13, +0.17, +0.16, +0.15 and +0.10 and against a month back +0.08, +0.10, +0.08, +0.02, -0.02 and -0.04 points; the ECB's spreads of the 10-year rate over the 3-month, 1-year and 2-year rates 0.77, 0.44 and 0.31 points; of the 2,550 days of the past ten years (from 2016-11-17) 99% had a lower 10-year rate than now, the median was 0.58%, the lowest -0.82% (2020-03-09) and the highest 3.63% (2026-09-28).
- **What the checks are and are not.** Limits were set from the whole record before any test. Plain checks (properties, not the ECB's sums, said so on the card): units (the ECB's labels: percent per annum), all fifteen series end on the same day, no hole of more than 8 days in the 10-year rate (real gaps 1 to 6 days), every spot rate between -3% and 10% (real -0.996 to 5.175) and every spread between -4 and 6 points (real -1.781 to 3.538), each series moving by no more than a bound set from its real one-day extremes (the 10-year rate -0.5 to +0.5 against a real -0.2646 to +0.2362; the 3-month rate -1.5 to +0.5 against -0.9397 to +0.2724), the six model parameters inside wide real ranges on the newest day. **The ECB's own arithmetic, said so on the card:** each published spread equals the 10-year rate minus the shorter one (the real gap is at most 1e-10 on all 5,675 days; tolerance 0.000001, applied to the last 520 days), and each spot rate equals the Svensson curve evaluated with the published parameters (the real gap is at most 1.5e-10 on 5,670 days; tolerance 0.000001, applied to the newest day only). They show that the series belong to one release, not that the figures are right.
- **Found while building it.** The curve identity does NOT hold on five old days (four in May 2007, off by up to 6e-5, and 2022-06-23, off by 0.056 points: that day's parameters carry 8 decimals instead of 15), so a check over a window would hide the card for the window's length after one such day: it is applied to the newest day only, and the card says so (loop-rules item 17, new). Tau 1 is above Tau 2 on 2,467 of the 5,675 days and below it on 3,208, so no ordering is checked. The first tests failed for four faults of my own, none in the module: a rounding world whose level did not separate one decimal from two, a one-day parameter series emptied by a test that meant to end the series, move messages written without the word "points", and a world whose step exceeded the move bound it was meant to stay inside.
- **Found by the breakage run.** 238 deliberate one-line changes (every constant, every limit on both sides, every comparison and the order of the checks, the sums and signs of both identities, the window, the week and month back, the rounding, the three requests, the cache times): 236 caught at first, 2 not, and both were about what the module REPORTS, not what it refuses: the reported largest spread gap lost its sign when a spread lay below its identity, and the curve check could have used the parameters of an older day if the source ever sent more than one. Two tests added. Second run: **238 of 238 caught, none unkillable** (the exact-in-binary edges 0.25, 0.5, 1.0 and a flat zero curve reached every equality-at-a-bound change, including all nine move bounds on both sides).
- **Verified.** 33 new tests in `test_euroyields.py` on a real fixture (the 10-year rate for 2,700 days, the eight other series for 520 days, the six parameters of the newest day and the raw layout of a real response): every figure worked by hand and in plain Python outside the module, every check on both sides of its limit on synthetic worlds where the other checks hold by construction, the order of the checks, the reach of each check (whole window, the last 520 days, the newest day only), the real window never refused and its two largest real 10-year moves pinned (+0.2362 on 2025-03-05, -0.2646 on 2023-03-15), the three requests and the `+` between series left unencoded. Whole backend suite 2,419 passed; phone typecheck clean, 78 unit tests, `export:web`; browser tests 62 of 62 with every live card present (a first run of the new card's scenario alone gave 31 of 31); the Macro accessibility probe measured 39 card source links (the smallest 45 points) with no violation; the top and the bottom of the card read in real screenshots; the module's live run (02:43 UTC) gave the same figures as the fixture.
- **Not verified.** The ECB's terms (the website host is refused here; its re-use policy is from memory); that a fitted curve describes the market (it is the ECB's model: the card says it is a fitted curve, not a quote); that a one-day move beyond the bounds would be shown (it would hide the card by design: the 10-year bound is about twice the largest real move); the card on a real phone and with a real screen reader. The note and the "Check:" paragraph of the card repeat some of the same words (as on the stress card); left as it is.
- **Credibly profitable?** Not applicable to this job (a data card describes yields that already happened, not a signal). Status: not yet.
- **Next candidates.** ECB bank lending (`BSI` loans), euro-area GDP (`ESTAT:MNA`), or the sovereign-stress series of the CISS dataflow. 03 UTC skills job: nothing new expected unless a new gap shows; 04 UTC strategy: no change unless the owner chooses a way out of 14e (`btcquant/DATA_OPTIONS.md`).
