## 2026-11-24 04:30 UTC (Claude, strategy job; owner mission): after the outage the five missed daily runs were caught up in one run (the first real stop-outs); no family run (the owner has not chosen a way out of 14e)
- **The outage and what it cost.** The session was down from about 2026-11-19 06:00 UTC to 2026-11-24 04:17 UTC (four days and 22 hours; the worker restarted over and over). 124 scheduled triggers queued up and were read together at 04:20 UTC: 119 hourly ones (2026-11-19 06:17 to 2026-11-24 04:17) and 5 daily ones (00:15 UTC on 11-20, 11-21, 11-22, 11-23 and 11-24). As the loop rules say, ONE hourly cycle was done for the current hour (this one), and the unfinished 05 UTC data job of 2026-11-19 was completed and committed (`f3c3901`, the entry before this one). **118 hourly cycles were therefore missed and are not made up** (a cycle is a unit of work, not a debt). Nothing was lost: the working tree, the branch and the remote were as the session left them.
- **The daily runs: five in one.** Run in a throwaway worktree of `origin/feature/research-modules` (the loop rules' way after an outage; nothing unpushed touched `btcquant`): `python3 -m atlas_api.btcquant.daily` at 04:21:55 UTC, one status line, exit 0: "no champions: the main portfolio is in cash; 18 accounts; data to 2026-11-23; update: 5 new candle(s) added". Protocol 1.5, frozen check empty, data not stale (2,884 candles, no gap, latest close 61,189.97), every account stepped by five days (15 accounts from 4 to 9 days, `monday-9db66957` from 2 to 7, `three_down-e7923e48` and `volume_surge-9c042d26` from 1 to 6), none halted, no breaker trip; 29 files changed, all under `atlas/btcquant` (data, state, reports, dashboard), committed once as `btcquant daily run 2026-11-24` (`e6bc324`) and pushed; the worktree removed and the main tree fast-forwarded with its uncommitted work intact. The five candles, UTC days: 11-19 close 61,913.23 (-1.70%); 11-20 open 61,911.58, low 57,655.17, close 58,814.99 (-5.01%); 11-21 open 58,815.33, low 55,106.00, close 60,086.00 (+2.16%); 11-22 close 61,163.01 (+1.79%); 11-23 close 61,189.97 (+0.04%).
- **The first real stop-outs, and what they show.** Until now no account had been stopped out on real data. On the 11-21 candle (open 58,815.33, low 55,106.00, 6.3% under the open, the close back at 60,086.00) four monitors were stopped out: `bollinger_mr-c251886b`, `regime_switch-9c030efe` and `rsi_oversold-a8d8143b` at a stop of 56,222.68 (filled at 56,194.57: the stop less the 0.05% slippage; fee 0.71) and `volume_surge-9c042d26` at 56,971.06 (filled at 56,942.58). Each lost 1.02% of its $10,000 (equity 9,898.13 and 9,898.11) where the exposure-matched hold lost 0.24% (0.22%): the stop did what it is written to do, and on this one episode it cost more than holding would have, because the price came back; one episode proves nothing either way. `three_down-e7923e48`: entry signalled at the 11-19 close (two lower closes), bought at the 11-20 open (61,942.54), exit signalled at the 11-21 close (the streak ended), sold at the 11-22 open (60,055.96): a round trip of -0.25% after costs (equity 9,974.89). `monday-9db66957`: bought at the Monday 11-23 open (61,193.59); its sell was signalled at that day's close and fills at the 11-24 open in the next run (00:15 UTC on 11-25). The two fully invested accounts (`buy_hold`, `vol_target-2dbe9ef1`): -4.71% since 2026-11-15, largest drawdown at a close 9.95% against the 15% line, so no breaker trip. Since each account opened (2026-11-15, 11-17 or 11-18), against buying and holding (-4.71%, -5.74% for `monday`, -5.82% for the two opened on 11-18): the ten accounts with no trade (`cash`, the $100,000 `portfolio` and eight monitors that never entered) 0.00%; the four stopped out -1.02%; `three_down` -0.25%; `monday` -0.11%. Read through `view.paper_view()` on the real state, the Journey payload words the new events ("STOP hit at $56,222.68", "SOLD 0.0126 BTC at $56,194.57 (stopped out); fee $0.71, slippage cost $0.35") with no gap in the wording (29 feed lines, the question card still present).
- **Second looks, all read-only, on the caught-up files.** Paper-account audit: 18 accounts, 13 fills and 154 curve days replayed against 2,884 stored candles (2019-01-01 to 2026-11-23), every difference 0, no problems: **the first time the audit replays real stop-outs** (four stop hits and their fills), which the entries of 2026-11-18 listed as not verified; the circuit breaker and a rejected order are still unseen on a real ledger. Second source (Hyperliquid's BTC perpetual, a perpetual future and not spot): 2,288 days in both files (2020-08-19 to 2026-11-23); median close difference 0.056%, 99% of days within 0.272%, largest -1.058% on 2023-03-12, 3 days over 0.5%, none over 2%; daily returns correlate at 0.99976 and have the same sign on 2,265 of 2,287; the newest common candle (2026-11-23) is 61,189.97 stored against 61,251.0 at the venue (+0.1%); no flags, so the five new days sit inside the same band. No separate live Coinbase look: the run itself had just fetched these candles. Rehearsal of the 2026-11-25 00:15 run with `python -m atlas_api.btcquant.rehearse` (stand-in for 11-24: the forming candle at 04:25 UTC, open 61,190.81, close so far 60,430.52, -1.24%): no problems; 18 accounts each one day on, none halted, no order waiting afterwards, 21 files changed and `reports/2026-11-25.md` added, the repeat identical but for the dashboard's counters, every file of the repository hashing equal before and after.
- **Checked.** Both triggers are enabled (the daily run `15 0 * * *`, next 2026-11-25 00:15 UTC; the hourly one `17 * * * *`, next 05:17 UTC; both bound to this session); the draft pull request (#7) is open, a draft, has no comments and no reviews, and its head is this branch's last commit; GitHub Actions on the newest commit: `mobile` succeeded on both runs of the newest commit (`f3c3901`) and `backend` was still running when this was written, two minutes after the push (its result is the first thing to read at 05 UTC); nobody else pushed (origin equalled the local head before each push); no module the daily run imports, no protocol, registry, strategy or plan file has changed since the 2026-11-19 04:21 rehearsal (the diff since `b1bae95` outside the run's own files touches none of them); `owner_question.json` is still there, unchanged since 2026-11-18.
- **No family run.** The owner has not chosen (waiting since 2026-11-17, seven days now; the question has been on the Journey tab since 2026-11-18: 1 lower the bar, 2 add data, 3 stop the daily queue, which is the recommendation; the answer comes in the chat, and then `btcquant/owner_question.json` is deleted): nothing was pre-registered or run, and no research, protocol or account file was changed by hand. The three schedules left in the queue stay DEFERRED (`btcquant/DATA_OPTIONS.md`).

No code changed this hour; the docs hygiene tests pass.

## 2026-11-24 05:04 UTC (Claude, improve job; found while verifying the 04 UTC cycle): the dealers card said "DOES NOT MATCH" because the New York Fed had revised one older week; the card now names the week, and the module's tests were hardened
- **What was seen.** The real `healthy` browser scenario passed 30 of 31 after the outage: its long Macro test stopped at the dealers card, which showed the red line "DOES NOT MATCH: the week-to-week changes differ from the source's own change figures." (the test fails the run if that phrase appears). It was not a regression: the check code dates from 2026-11-10 and the dealers files were last edited on 2026-11-17 (the shared `SourceLink`), and the same scenario passed 31 of 31 on 2026-11-19 with this card on live data, so the source changed between then and now.
- **Cause, found by comparing the saved download of 2026-11-10 with a fresh one of 2026-11-24 row by row** (the fresh one is saved as `tests/fixtures/nyfed_pd_treasury_20261124.json`, trimmed to September 2024 on, 115 weeks). Exactly ONE level row differs: 2026-10-21 went from 425,643 to 411,822 million (-13,821). Two change figures differ: that week's went from -17,855 to -17,132 (+723, not the -13,821 the level moved) and the next week's, 2026-10-28, from 27,116 to 40,937 (+13,821, exactly the level's move). So for 2026-10-21 alone the levels now give -31,676 while the source's own change figure says -17,132, a gap of 14.5 billion; the other 25 of the 26 weeks fit and the latest week (2026-11-11, 456.0 billion, down 11.3) fits. The code was right and the source disagrees with itself; which of its two figures is the revised truth cannot be told from here.
- **Decision, written before any code.** Nothing is loosened and no week is dropped: the 1.0 million tolerance stays and the card still shows the disagreement. The red alarm stays for the week the card SHOWS (its change must equal the source's own change figure). An older week that the source no longer fits with itself is named in neutral words with both figures and the plain sentence that the card cannot tell which is right. A server that does not send the new fields (an older one) keeps the old red line.
- **Built, tests first.** Backend `dealers.summarize`: the check keeps `weeks`, `mismatches` and `match` (all 26 weeks) and gains `latest_match` (does the week shown fit) and `bad_weeks` (up to three disagreeing weeks, newest first, each with both figures). Phone: `src/lib/dealers.ts` (`dealerCheck`: the words and whether they are red, pure so a unit test covers it, plus the date and figure helpers the card already had) used by `DealersCard.tsx`; `types.ts` gains the two fields; the browser test keeps the strict `noMatch(/DOES NOT MATCH/)` for the week shown and now ALSO requires a "Checked:" line, and, when it is the older-week form, its exact shape (the week named, both figures). On live data today the card reads "Checked: the latest week's change equals the source's own change figure. In 1 of the last 26 weeks the source's own figures do not fit each other (week of Oct 21, 2026: its levels give -$31.7B, its change figure says -$17.1B); this card cannot tell which of the two is right." in the normal text colour.
- **Found while testing (all older than today).** (a) The 26 pairs of weeks were built with `zip(dates[-27:-1], dates[-26:])`, which pairs each date with ITSELF when fewer than 27 dates are held (the parser accepts 10): such a response would have shown the red alarm with nothing compared. The real history has about 700 weeks, so it never happened; it now pairs neighbours first and takes the last 26. (b) `parse` raised an AttributeError, not a ValueError, on a JSON list, a string or `{"pd": "x"}`, so the card would have errored instead of saying it could not read the source; every other shape now ends as "not enough weeks". (c) The first breakage run of the module (152 one-line changes) caught 97 and let 55 through: the old tests did not look at the request address or the client name, the source and page text, the 16-day limit between current and overdue, the 104 weeks of history, the three-day window for the year-ago week, the 26-week reach of the check or what it does with a date in the middle of a week, a week missing from either series, how long a good answer (6 hours) or a failed one (2 minutes) is kept, the clock the age is counted from, which transport errors become the plain failure sentence, or whether the client the service made is closed. Twenty-three tests closed them (the file went from 11 tests to 39: five for the new check and the real download, twenty-three for the gaps).
- **Verified.** Backend: `tests/test_dealers.py` 39 tests (11 before), all passing, five of them written first for the new check and watched failing before the code changed. Breakage run of `dealers.py`: 149 one-line changes (constants, the parser, the 26-week check, the year-ago and range figures, the age, the history, the service), all 149 caught in 199 s, the file restored and its hash checked; the table was run three times: 152 changes caught 97, after 23 tests and two fixes a table rewritten for the new pairing (147 changes) caught 146, and one more test closed the last survivor (the final table has 149 because two entries moved in from the equivalent list: the new three-day test catches them). The six equivalent entries (5 kinds: pairing with the full list instead of its first part, because `zip` stops at the shorter; the match flag without its "at least one pair" test, which the check can never be built without; the last instead of the first of two dates in the year-ago window, which weekly dates never give; `>=` for `>` at exactly 365 days; dropping `TypeError` or `KeyError` from the failure handler, which nothing past `parse` raises) were run on their own: all 6 survive, as they must. Phone: type check clean; 91 unit tests (79 and 12 new for the wording, the figures and the date helper); a breakage run of `lib/dealers.ts` (50 changes: 48 caught, 2 equivalent: moving the clock time inside the same UTC day) after five gaps were closed (a negative zero, the separator between weeks, a count of "more" that must never go negative, the formatting digits, and the time zone, which needs a test that sets `TZ` to a zone 14 hours ahead and one 11 behind). Whole backend suite: 3,663 passed, exit 0, no failures or skips (the 3,635 of the JOLTS files plus the 28 new dealers tests; run while the breakage run shared the machine, so no duration is given). Browser: the `healthy` scenario 31 of 31 on today's live data (it was 30 of 31 before the fix), then the whole run 62 of 62 including the accessibility gate, both on the new web export, with the footer "Every live card had data this run, so every content check ran" (so the dealers checks ran on the revised data: the red phrase is absent, a "Checked:" line is present and has the older-week shape). Screenshot read: the dealers card on live data shows the neutral sentence in the normal small grey type, names the week (Oct 21, 2026) and both figures (-$31.7B and -$17.1B), wraps cleanly in four lines, and nothing else on the card moved; no defect found.
- **Not verified.** The card on a real phone; which of the source's two figures is right (the card says it cannot tell); whether the source will revise the week again (the line then reads "Checked: ... equal ... for the last 26 weeks" by itself, and the new fixture and tests keep the case); the older-server red line in a browser (only in the unit test); the other cards after a failed Macro check in a future run: one failed assertion still ends that whole test and hides every card after it (loop-rules item 26, LOOP.md backlog).
- **Credibly profitable?** Not applicable (a data card, no strategy was tested). Status: not yet.
- **Next candidates.** 05 UTC data (the next hourly cycle): the MIR rates on outstanding amounts, real consumer spending against nominal and the price index (series notes first: BEA derives them), or the JOLTS hires, quits and layoffs by industry (probe the ids first). 06 UTC improve: independent card checks in the Macro browser test (LOOP.md backlog, found here), the breakage harness in the repo (written ten times by now; run on a copy of the backend), the Silver futures check, the clipped `fail_reason` messages in the older providers. 07 UTC strategy: no change unless the owner decides 14e (`btcquant/DATA_OPTIONS.md`). 00:15 UTC the daily run.
